The reliance on LLMs can introduce biases and requires careful handling to ensure fairness and avoid reinforcing stereotypes. The simulation of image analysis with AI is an interesting factor, but could be biased. This is an inherent risk in using this framework and the authors address this.  Future research could explore more sophisticated emotion modeling, incorporate more diverse user characteristics, and investigate the ethical implications of using synthetic users.
    *   Lack of comparison to other simulated user frameworks (besides RecAgent and Agent4Rec), is a missed opportunity to demonstrate the benefits of the approach.
    *   Limited explanation for the choice of parameters (K1, K2, alpha).

*   **Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly written. The methodology is well-explained, and the experiments are carefully designed. The results are presented in a clear and concise manner. However, more details on the dataset characteristics would be helpful.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Score: 8

Justification:

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of recommender systems by providing a novel and practical framework for simulating user behavior using LLMs. The integration of personas, visual cues, knowledge graphs, and causal reasoning mechanisms makes SimUSER a more realistic and believable human proxy compared to previous approaches. The experimental results demonstrate the framework's effectiveness in bridging the evaluation gap and providing valuable insights into user engagement. The ability to align with A/B testing results is also a huge win. The impact of incorporating visual signals and other features is significant. The main shortcomings are a lack of comparison to other methods, and certain choices in parameter values could have benefitted from more justification. The biases inherent to LLM techniques and the limited availability of interaction data pose some constraints, these are well acknowledged by the authors. Overall, SimUSER shows very strong, real-world potential and is a valuable tool.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Validating LLM-Generated Relevance Labels for Educational Resource Search
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12732v1)
- **Authors**: Ratan J. Sebastian, Anett Hoppe
- **Abstract**: Manual relevance judgements in Information Retrieval are costly and require expertise, driving interest in using Large Language Models (LLMs) for automatic assessment. While LLMs have shown promise in general web search scenarios, their effectiveness for evaluating domain-specific search results, such as educational resources, remains unexplored. To investigate different ways of including domain-specific criteria in LLM prompts for relevance judgement, we collected and released a dataset of 401 human relevance judgements from a user study involving teaching professionals performing search tasks related to lesson planning. We compared three approaches to structuring these prompts: a simple two-aspect evaluation baseline from prior work on using LLMs as relevance judges, a comprehensive 12-dimensional rubric derived from educational literature, and criteria directly informed by the study participants. Using domain-specific frameworks, LLMs achieved strong agreement with human judgements (Cohen's $\kappa$ up to 0.650), significantly outperforming the baseline approach. The participant-derived framework proved particularly robust, with GPT-3.5 achieving $\kappa$ scores of 0.639 and 0.613 for 10-dimension and 5-dimension versions respectively. System-level evaluation showed that LLM judgements reliably identified top-performing retrieval approaches (RBO scores 0.71-0.76) while maintaining reasonable discrimination between systems (RBO 0.52-0.56). These findings suggest that LLMs can effectively evaluate educational resources when prompted with domain-specific criteria, though performance varies with framework complexity and input structure.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate relevance labels for educational resources. The authors compare different prompt structures for LLMs, incorporating domain-specific criteria based on educational literature and user study findings. They evaluate LLM performance by comparing agreement with human judgments and assessing the impact on system-level evaluation metrics. The results suggest that LLMs can effectively evaluate educational resources when prompted with domain-specific criteria, particularly those derived from user studies, outperforming prompts based on more generic relevance aspects.  However, the study also notes variations in performance depending on the LLM used and the complexity of the relevance framework, and some differences in performance between assessment of query difficulty versus judging retrieval systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper makes a novel contribution by specifically examining the application of LLMs for relevance judgments in the domain of educational resource search, a relatively unexplored area compared to general web search. It builds upon existing work on LLM-based relevance assessment but extends it to a complex professional domain with unique relevance criteria.  The comparison of different prompt structures, including those derived from user studies, adds to the novelty.
* **Significance:**  The research has potential significance for improving the efficiency and scalability of educational resource evaluation. Manually creating relevance judgments is time-consuming and expensive.  If LLMs can automate this process effectively, it could significantly reduce the burden on human evaluators and enable more rapid development and improvement of educational search systems. The paper also provides valuable insights into the importance of domain-specific knowledge when using LLMs for relevance assessment. It also contributes to our understanding of best practices for implementing LLM-based IR evaluation.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined Research Questions:** The paper clearly articulates two key research questions that guide the investigation.
    * **Rigorous Methodology:** The study employs a thorough methodology, including a user study to collect ground-truth data, the design and implementation of different LLM-based assessment frameworks, and a comprehensive evaluation using both document-level and system-level metrics.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The analysis is detailed and explores the impact of different prompt structures, input representations, and LLMs on relevance judgment performance. The dimensional contribution analysis provides valuable insights into how LLMs are assessing educational resources.
    * **Dataset Availability:**  The authors have made their dataset publicly available, which can facilitate further research in this area.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to high school STEM teachers in Germany. The generalizability of the findings to other educational contexts and subject areas needs further investigation.
    * **Input Modalities:** Performance was lower in the Image input modality, suggesting more work is needed on how to optimize performance in this area.
    * **Frameworks Tested:** While the paper compares multiple frameworks, there remains the potential to optimize frameworks further to identify the ideal combination of dimensions.
    * **General Performance:** While the best performance approached human judgement, it did not consistently exceed them, limiting the level of automation that could be achieved.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM-based relevance assessment, particularly in domain-specific applications. The insights into the importance of domain knowledge and the effectiveness of user-derived prompts can guide the design of more effective LLM-based evaluation systems.  The methodology provides a useful framework for future research in this area. The released dataset can also support further experimentation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution that tackles a relevant and timely problem with a rigorous methodology and detailed analysis. It is novel in its focus on educational resource search and the investigation of domain-specific prompts. While the findings are somewhat limited by the scope of the evaluation, the paper provides valuable insights and guidance for future research in this area. Taking both strengths and weaknesses into account and its implications in the context of the current landscape, a score of 7 is appropriate. This reflects the solid contribution of the paper with areas identified that would increase its significance with further research.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mask Image Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12739v1)
- **Authors**: Runyi Hu, Jie Zhang, Shiqian Zhao, Nils Lukas, Jiwei Li, Qing Guo, Han Qiu, Tianwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: We present MaskMark, a simple, efficient and flexible framework for image watermarking. MaskMark has two variants: MaskMark-D, which supports global watermark embedding, watermark localization, and local watermark extraction for applications such as tamper detection, and MaskMark-ED, which focuses on local watermark embedding and extraction with enhanced robustness in small regions, enabling localized image protection. Built upon the classical Encoder- Distortion-Decoder training paradigm, MaskMark-D introduces a simple masking mechanism during the decoding stage to support both global and local watermark extraction. A mask is applied to the watermarked image before extraction, allowing the decoder to focus on selected regions and learn local extraction. A localization module is also integrated into the decoder to identify watermark regions during inference, reducing interference from irrelevant content and improving accuracy. MaskMark-ED extends this design by incorporating the mask into the encoding stage as well, guiding the encoder to embed the watermark in designated local regions for enhanced robustness. Comprehensive experiments show that MaskMark achieves state-of-the-art performance in global watermark extraction, local watermark extraction, watermark localization, and multi-watermark embedding. It outperforms all existing baselines, including the recent leading model WAM for local watermarking, while preserving high visual quality of the watermarked images. MaskMark is also flexible, by adjusting the distortion layer, it can adapt to different robustness requirements with just a few steps of fine-tuning. Moreover, our approach is efficient and easy to optimize, requiring only 20 hours on a single A6000 GPU with just 1/15 the computational cost of WAM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mask Image Watermarking":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MaskMark, a new framework for image watermarking that supports both global and local watermark embedding, extraction, and tamper localization. MaskMark has two variants: MaskMark-D (for global embedding and local extraction) and MaskMark-ED (for local embedding and extraction with enhanced robustness). The core idea is to use a masking mechanism during the decoding phase (MaskMark-D) or both encoding and decoding phases (MaskMark-ED) to guide the watermark extraction process. This allows the decoder to focus on specific regions and improve robustness against distortions.  The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance in various watermarking tasks and highlight the efficiency and flexibility of their approach.  They also emphasize its adaptability to different robustness requirements and attack scenarios via fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a masking mechanism to guide the extraction process is a reasonable and potentially effective approach.  While not groundbreaking, the introduction of a dedicated localization module and the differentiation between MaskMark-D and MaskMark-ED offer some degree of novelty. The idea of using the mask during the encoding stage (MaskMark-ED) to focus watermark strength is a sound extension. The emphasis on local watermarking, where the ability to embed and extract from specific regions is crucial, makes the contribution pertinent.

*   **Significance:** The reported performance gains over existing methods, particularly in local watermarking scenarios and under geometric distortions, are significant. The efficiency of the framework, requiring less computation than WAM, could contribute to the practicality of using it in real-world applications. The scalability to larger bit lengths is a definite plus. The demonstrated ability to fine-tune the model for specific attack scenarios, such as VAE-based attacks, increases the framework's relevance and practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation covering different tasks (global, local, multi-watermarking, tamper localization).
    *   Demonstrated state-of-the-art performance.
    *   Emphasis on efficiency and flexibility, enhancing practical use.
    *   Strong experimental ablation that thoroughly justifies design choices.
    *   Good comparison against recent baselines, including ablation tests.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The core masking mechanism, while effective, isn't conceptually revolutionary. It's an incremental improvement upon existing encoder-decoder watermarking architectures.
    *   While the results are impressive, the claims of "significant" outperformance may be slightly overstated in some cases, especially when the gains are only marginal.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more thorough analysis of the limitations. For example, do the gains achieved by masking come at a cost in terms of overall watermark capacity or imperceptibility?

*   **Potential Impact:**  If the results are reproducible and translate well to real-world applications, MaskMark has the potential to influence the field of image watermarking, particularly in scenarios where local watermarking, tamper detection, and robustness are critical.  The efficiency and adaptability could encourage wider adoption.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-engineered and thoroughly evaluated watermarking framework with a solid technical contribution. The masking mechanism and localization module offer tangible benefits, especially in local watermarking and robustness to distortions. The emphasis on efficiency and flexibility significantly increases the practical value of the work.

However, the core idea isn't radically new, and the performance gains, while valuable, aren't always dramatically higher than existing methods in *every* scenario. Also, I don't see any discussion about the tradeoff between imperceptibility/capacity vs. Robustness with masking.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Privacy Protection Against Personalized Text-to-Image Synthesis via Cross-image Consistency Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12747v1)
- **Authors**: Guanyu Wang, Kailong Wang, Yihao Huang, Mingyi Zhou, Zhang Qing cnwatcher, Geguang Pu, Li Li
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of diffusion models and personalization techniques has made it possible to recreate individual portraits from just a few publicly available images. While such capabilities empower various creative applications, they also introduce serious privacy concerns, as adversaries can exploit them to generate highly realistic impersonations. To counter these threats, anti-personalization methods have been proposed, which add adversarial perturbations to published images to disrupt the training of personalization models. However, existing approaches largely overlook the intrinsic multi-image nature of personalization and instead adopt a naive strategy of applying perturbations independently, as commonly done in single-image settings. This neglects the opportunity to leverage inter-image relationships for stronger privacy protection. Therefore, we advocate for a group-level perspective on privacy protection against personalization. Specifically, we introduce Cross-image Anti-Personalization (CAP), a novel framework that enhances resistance to personalization by enforcing style consistency across perturbed images. Furthermore, we develop a dynamic ratio adjustment strategy that adaptively balances the impact of the consistency loss throughout the attack iterations. Extensive experiments on the classical CelebHQ and VGGFace2 benchmarks show that CAP substantially improves existing methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the privacy concerns arising from the use of personalized text-to-image synthesis techniques, which can reconstruct individual portraits from limited data. It proposes a novel framework called Cross-image Anti-Personalization (CAP).  CAP enhances resistance to personalization by enforcing style consistency across perturbed images.  A dynamic ratio adjustment strategy balances the impact of consistency and reconstruction losses during the adversarial attack. Experimental results on CelebA-HQ and VGGFace2 datasets demonstrate CAP's effectiveness in improving existing anti-personalization methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in reframing anti-personalization as a cross-image privacy protection task. The idea of explicitly modeling and leveraging inter-image consistency to thwart personalization attacks is novel. Existing methods treat each image independently, which the authors argue is a crucial oversight given that personalization models learn from multiple images. The dynamic ratio adjustment strategy for balancing losses is also a contribution.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant and increasingly important with the proliferation of personalized generative models. The ability to generate realistic impersonations has serious implications for privacy and security. The CAP framework offers a potentially effective defense against this threat. The experimental results, showing improvements over existing methods (Anti-DreamBooth and SimAC), support the significance of the work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem formulation and motivation.
    *   The cross-image consistency approach is well-reasoned and theoretically sound.
    *   The dynamic ratio adjustment strategy is a useful addition that improves performance.
    *   The paper is well-written and easy to understand.
    *   The experimental setup is thorough and includes appropriate baselines and evaluation metrics.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of CAP.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on Gram matrices for style representation might be limiting.  While widely used, Gram matrices capture only texture information and may not be robust to more complex style variations.
    *   The method's sensitivity to the selection of layer(s) used for feature extraction is not thoroughly explored and could require significant tuning for optimal results.
    *   The computational cost of computing Gram matrices may be a practical concern.
    *   The effectiveness of CAP in more challenging scenarios, such as those involving varying poses, expressions, or lighting conditions, is not adequately evaluated.
    *   Although the experiments are thorough, the scope of datasets tested is somewhat narrow. Extending to more diverse datasets could further prove the robustness of the approach.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper can influence the development of more robust privacy protection methods for generative models.  The CAP framework can be integrated into existing approaches to enhance their effectiveness.  It also highlights the importance of considering inter-image relationships in privacy protection.

*   **Score:** 7.5

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of privacy protection in generative models. The cross-image consistency idea is novel and well-motivated. The improvements over existing methods, demonstrated through experiments, are significant. While there are some limitations, such as the choice of Gram matrices for style representation and lack of robustness verification using more diverse datasets, the paper offers a practical and theoretically sound approach that has the potential to make a positive impact on the field. It earns a score of 7.5 due to its novelty, practical utility, and clear presentation despite some shortcomings in robustness validation.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Trajectory Adaptation using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12755v1)
- **Authors**: Anurag Maurya, Tashmoy Ghosh, Ravi Prakash
- **Abstract**: Adapting robot trajectories based on human instructions as per new situations is essential for achieving more intuitive and scalable human-robot interactions. This work proposes a flexible language-based framework to adapt generic robotic trajectories produced by off-the-shelf motion planners like RRT, A-star, etc, or learned from human demonstrations. We utilize pre-trained LLMs to adapt trajectory waypoints by generating code as a policy for dense robot manipulation, enabling more complex and flexible instructions than current methods. This approach allows us to incorporate a broader range of commands, including numerical inputs. Compared to state-of-the-art feature-based sequence-to-sequence models which require training, our method does not require task-specific training and offers greater interpretability and more effective feedback mechanisms. We validate our approach through simulation experiments on the robotic manipulator, aerial vehicle, and ground robot in the Pybullet and Gazebo simulation environments, demonstrating that LLMs can successfully adapt trajectories to complex human instructions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a natural language-based framework for adapting robotic trajectories. The core idea is to leverage Large Language Models (LLMs) to interpret human instructions and generate code that modifies existing trajectories. This code acts as a policy for dense robot manipulation, enabling complex and flexible instructions. The approach aims to avoid explicit mathematical formulations and task-specific training, offering greater interpretability and more effective feedback mechanisms. The method is validated in simulation experiments involving robotic manipulators, aerial vehicles, and ground robots, demonstrating LLMs' ability to adapt trajectories to complex human instructions. The paper proposes an iterative feedback loop where the user can provide refinements to the structured high-level plan.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonable approach by using LLMs for trajectory adaptation. While using LLMs for robotics is not entirely new, the focus on code generation as a policy for trajectory adaptation and the iterative feedback loop are worth noting.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential significance lies in its ability to create a more intuitive and scalable human-robot interaction. The use of natural language commands can simplify complex tasks. Moreover, the approach avoids the need for explicit mathematical models and task-specific training, which could lower the barrier to entry for non-expert users. The closed loop iterative feedback would lead to a better user interface than existing approaches.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The approach is flexible and can handle complex instructions, including numerical inputs.
    *   The iterative feedback mechanism allows for user-driven refinement of the trajectory, addressing potential misinterpretations.
    *   The method does not require task-specific training, which increases its generalizability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper relies heavily on LLMs, which can be unpredictable and may not always generate accurate code.
    *   The lack of mathematical guarantees means that the generated trajectories may not always be smooth or optimal.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on simulation experiments, and it is unclear how well the approach would perform in the real world.
    *   The performance might be highly dependent on the quality of the prompt engineering and the capabilities of the specific LLM used. The paper does not delve deeply into these prompt design challenges and choices.
    *   The novelty lies more in the application and integration of existing techniques (LLMs, code generation) rather than the development of fundamentally new algorithms or models for trajectory planning itself.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves to be robust and reliable in real-world scenarios, it could have a significant impact on the field of human-robot interaction. It could make it easier for non-expert users to program robots for complex tasks, thereby increasing the adoption of robots in various industries.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to trajectory adaptation. However, it primarily leverages existing technologies and does not introduce groundbreaking new algorithms or models. The reliance on LLMs, while potentially advantageous, also introduces uncertainties and limitations. The validation is limited to simulation experiments, and the real-world performance is unknown. Given these factors, the novelty lies in the composition of LLMs and the closed loop iteration which makes the approach worth noting. Therefore, a score of 6 seems appropriate.

**Score: 6**
- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### GraphOmni: A Comprehensive and Extendable Benchmark Framework for Large Language Models on Graph-theoretic Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12764v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Xu, Xiangru Jian, Xinjian Zhao, Wei Pang, Chao Zhang, Suyuchen Wang, Qixin Zhang, Joao Monteiro, Qiuzhuang Sun, Tianshu Yu
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we presented GraphOmni, a comprehensive benchmark framework for systematically evaluating the graph reasoning capabilities of LLMs. By analyzing critical dimensions, including graph types, serialization formats, and prompt schemes, we provided extensive insights into the strengths and limitations of current LLMs. Our empirical findings emphasize that no single serialization or prompting strategy consistently outperforms others. Motivated by these insights, we propose a reinforcement learning-based approach that dynamically selects the best serialization-prompt pairings, resulting in significant accuracy improvements. GraphOmni's modular and extensible design establishes a robust foundation for future research, facilitating advancements toward general-purpose graph reasoning models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GRAPHOMNI: A Comprehensive and Extendable Benchmark Framework for Large Language Models on Graph-theoretic Tasks":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GRAPHOMNI, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to reason about graph-theoretic tasks articulated in natural language. The benchmark encompasses diverse graph types (Erdős-Rényi, Barabási-Albert, etc.), serialization formats (adjacency lists, matrices, edge sets), and prompting schemes (algorithmic, chain-of-thought, k-shot). The authors perform an extensive evaluation of state-of-the-art LLMs, identifying critical interactions between these dimensions and their impact on model performance. The study reveals performance variability depending on serialization formats, prompting schemes, and graph types. Finally, the paper proposes a reinforcement learning-inspired adaptive mechanism to select optimal serialization-prompt pairings for enhanced LLM performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** GRAPHOMNI stands out due to its comprehensive nature, covering a wide range of graph types, serialization methods, and prompting schemes. This is a significant improvement over previous works that often focus on individual aspects.
    *   **Extendable and Flexible Design:** The benchmark is designed to be easily extendable, allowing researchers to incorporate new graph generators, serialization methods, and prompt strategies. This modularity is a key strength.
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The authors conduct a systematic evaluation of LLMs, identifying critical interactions among the benchmark dimensions and demonstrating their impact on model performance.
    *   **Practical Findings and Methods:** The paper provides insights into how serialization and prompting schemes affect performance. The RL-based adaptive mechanism offers a promising approach to optimizing LLM performance on graph reasoning tasks.
    *   **Random Baseline:** It is good that they have a implemented a random baseline in order to evaluate the models fairly.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Real-world Graph Data:** The benchmark focuses on synthetic graphs. While this allows for controlled experiments, it may not fully capture the complexities of real-world graph data. The authors could consider incorporating a small amount of real-world graph datasets to bridge the gap.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** While the paper claims scalability, there's no explicit discussion regarding the computational cost of evaluating LLMs across the entire benchmark, particularly with the adaptive mechanism. Further research may have to focus on this aspect.
    *   **Error Analysis Detail:** Although some preliminary error analysis has been performed, there's a lot of room to improve by giving in more detail.
    *   **Limited Baselines:** I think it would have been a better benchmark if the authors included one of the GNN model.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a comprehensive and extendable benchmark for evaluating LLMs on graph-theoretic tasks. The systematic evaluation of different dimensions and the identification of critical interactions contribute to a better understanding of LLM performance on structured reasoning tasks. The RL-based adaptive mechanism offers a practical approach to enhancing LLM performance and provides a foundation for future research in this area. However, the reliance on synthetic datasets and the absence of direct comparisons with specialized graph models (GNNs) limits the overall impact of the paper.
*   **Potential Influence:** GRAPHOMNI has the potential to become a valuable resource for the LLM research community, facilitating advancements in graph reasoning models. The extendable design and the systematic evaluation of different dimensions make it a useful tool for researchers to compare and evaluate new LLM architectures, serialization methods, and prompting strategies.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field, mainly due to the systematic approach of evaluating different dimensions and the introduction of an adaptive method for optimization. However, it lacks some of the rigor needed for a higher score, as it excludes real-world examples or comparison with traditional GNN models. Also, there needs more analysis about model error. The inclusion of real-world data and a direct comparison with GNNs would have significantly increased the paper's impact and demonstrated the strengths and weaknesses of LLMs in graph reasoning more explicitly.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing the Geometric Problem-Solving Ability of Multimodal LLMs via Symbolic-Neural Integration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12773v1)
- **Authors**: Yicheng Pan, Zhenrong Zhang, Pengfei Hu, Jiefeng Ma, Jun Du, Jianshu Zhang, Quan Liu, Jianqing Gao, Feng Ma
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved remarkable progress in general domains and demonstrated promise in multimodal mathematical reasoning. However, applying MLLMs to geometry problem solving (GPS) remains challenging due to lack of accurate step-by-step solution data and severe hallucinations during reasoning. In this paper, we propose GeoGen, a pipeline that can automatically generates step-wise reasoning paths for geometry diagrams. By leveraging the precise symbolic reasoning, \textbf{GeoGen} produces large-scale, high-quality question-answer pairs. To further enhance the logical reasoning ability of MLLMs, we train \textbf{GeoLogic}, a Large Language Model (LLM) using synthetic data generated by GeoGen. Serving as a bridge between natural language and symbolic systems, GeoLogic enables symbolic tools to help verifying MLLM outputs, making the reasoning process more rigorous and alleviating hallucinations. Experimental results show that our approach consistently improves the performance of MLLMs, achieving remarkable results on benchmarks for geometric reasoning tasks. This improvement stems from our integration of the strengths of LLMs and symbolic systems, which enables a more reliable and interpretable approach for the GPS task. Codes are available at https://github.com/ycpNotFound/GeoGen.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this paper.

**Summary**

The paper addresses the challenge of applying Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to Geometry Problem Solving (GPS). The authors identify two key limitations: the scarcity of high-quality, step-by-step solution data and the prevalence of hallucinations in MLLM reasoning. To overcome these issues, they propose GeoGen, a pipeline that automatically generates step-wise reasoning paths for geometry diagrams. GeoGen leverages precise symbolic reasoning to produce high-quality question-answer pairs, which are then used to train GeoLogic, an LLM that acts as a bridge between natural language and symbolic systems. GeoLogic facilitates the verification of MLLM outputs using symbolic tools, improving reasoning rigor and reducing hallucinations. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that this approach consistently enhances MLLM performance on geometric reasoning benchmarks. They integrate their training pipeline and data with existing MLLMs via SFT, leading to improvements in geometric reasoning ability in benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation**

Here's a detailed evaluation of the paper's novelty and significance:

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Specific Weakness of MLLMs:** The paper directly tackles a known weakness of MLLMs, i.e., hallucination and lack of step-by-step reasoning especially in complex, visually dependent tasks like geometry.
    *   **Automated Data Generation Pipeline:** GeoGen is a significant contribution. Generating high-quality training data for MLLMs is a major bottleneck, and the automated pipeline, utilizing both symbolic reasoning and LLMs to translate into natural language, is a valuable approach. This avoids the biases and limitations of template-based data generation and the unreliability of LLM-only generated solutions.
    *   **Symbolic-Neural Integration:** The idea of using GeoLogic as a bridge for verification is sound. It allows the MLLM to leverage the strengths of symbolic systems for logical rigor and hallucination mitigation. The step-level verification is a crucial improvement over simple answer checking.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The paper features comprehensive experimental evaluation, including ablation studies to demonstrate the effectiveness of different components (GeoExpand, GeoSynth, GeoLogic). They evaluated it on multiple LLMs and against baselines.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and structured. The approach is clearly explained, and the experimental results are presented logically.

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **GeoGen Pipeline:** The construction of the GeoGen is quite novel. It is leveraging both symbolic and neural approaches to generate CoT data automatically. This differs from existing method which rely solely on either LLM or symbolic systems.
    *   **GeoLogic as a Bridge:** The use of an LLM (GeoLogic) to translate between the natural language and formal language is a novel concept. By enabling MLLMs to reason over a symbolic space, it allows for more verifiable inferences to be made.
    *   **Step-Level Verification:** This is another key novel aspect of the paper. They use tree-based search, and verify at each level.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Improved GPS Performance:** The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in performance on standard geometry benchmarks, suggesting that the approach is effective.
    *   **Contribution to Symbolic-Neural Integration:** The paper provides a valuable example of successful symbolic-neural integration for a complex reasoning task, highlighting the potential of this paradigm for improving MLLM performance.
    *   **Potential for Generalization:** While the paper focuses on geometry, the underlying principles of automated data generation and symbolic verification could be extended to other complex, visually dependent reasoning tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Symbolic Systems:** While the paper leverages FormalGeo effectively, the overall performance is limited by the expressiveness and coverage of the symbolic system. Further improvements to symbolic systems will be needed to fully realize the potential of the integrated approach.
    *   **Scalability of Verification:** The step-level verification process could be computationally expensive, especially for more complex problems requiring longer reasoning chains. Efficient implementation and optimization strategies are needed to address this issue. The paper mentions this but doesn't fully explore it.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Generalization:** While the paper provides results on GeoTest, it could benefit from more extensive evaluation on diverse and challenging datasets to assess the generalization ability of the approach. While they evaluated on MathVista, the sample size is too small.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** The work integrates existing components (LLMs, symbolic solvers) into a novel pipeline. While the integration is significant, the individual components aren't groundbreaking in themselves.

*   **Overall:** The integration of symbolic reasoning to assist neural approaches is crucial for solving complex reasoning tasks. Generating data automatically has solved the problem of insufficient high-quality training data and enables symbolic verification. They achieved this by developing GeoGen and training GeoLogic and fine-tuning the MLLMs. Therefore, the overall impact on GPS is significant.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of geometric problem solving by introducing a novel pipeline for automated data generation and symbolic-neural integration. GeoGen and GeoLogic effectively address the key limitations of MLLMs in this domain, leading to improved performance and more interpretable reasoning. The experiments are thorough, and the paper is well-written. The 7.5 score reflects the significant contributions, novelty of the pipeline, but also acknowledges the weaknesses like incremental contribution in general, heavy reliance on existing symbolic solvers, scalability concerns for very complex problems, and limited evaluation of the generalization ability. However, this paper presents a vital step for future research of symbolic and neural integration.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EarthGPT-X: Enabling MLLMs to Flexibly and Comprehensively Understand Multi-Source Remote Sensing Imagery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12795v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Zhang, Miaoxin Cai, Yaqian Ning, Tong Zhang, Yin Zhuang, He Chen, Jun Li, Xuerui Mao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in the visual-language area have developed natural multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) for spatial reasoning through visual prompting. However, due to remote sensing (RS) imagery containing abundant geospatial information that differs from natural images, it is challenging to effectively adapt natural spatial models to the RS domain. Moreover, current RS MLLMs are limited in overly narrow interpretation levels and interaction manner, hindering their applicability in real-world scenarios. To address those challenges, a spatial MLLM named EarthGPT-X is proposed, enabling a comprehensive understanding of multi-source RS imagery, such as optical, synthetic aperture radar (SAR), and infrared. EarthGPT-X offers zoom-in and zoom-out insight, and possesses flexible multi-grained interactive abilities. Moreover, EarthGPT-X unifies two types of critical spatial tasks (i.e., referring and grounding) into a visual prompting framework. To achieve these versatile capabilities, several key strategies are developed. The first is the multi-modal content integration method, which enhances the interplay between images, visual prompts, and text instructions. Subsequently, a cross-domain one-stage fusion training strategy is proposed, utilizing the large language model (LLM) as a unified interface for multi-source multi-task learning. Furthermore, by incorporating a pixel perception module, the referring and grounding tasks are seamlessly unified within a single framework. In addition, the experiments conducted demonstrate the superiority of the proposed EarthGPT-X in multi-grained tasks and its impressive flexibility in multi-modal interaction, revealing significant advancements of MLLM in the RS field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the EarthGPT-X paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EarthGPT-X, a novel spatial multi-modal large language model (MLLM) designed for comprehensive understanding of multi-source remote sensing (RS) imagery, including optical, SAR, and infrared data. A key focus is enabling flexible interaction and achieving both fine-grained ("zoom-in") and coarse-grained ("zoom-out") reasoning capabilities.  EarthGPT-X unifies referring and grounding tasks into a visual prompting framework. The approach involves: (1) a multi-modal content integration method to enhance the interplay between images, visual prompts, and text instructions; (2) a cross-domain one-stage fusion training strategy using a large language model (LLM) as a unified interface for multi-source, multi-task learning; (3) a pixel perception module to unify referring and grounding tasks. The paper also introduces a new multi-source visual prompt-based instruction dataset, M-RSVP.  Experimental results demonstrate EarthGPT-X's superiority in multi-grained tasks and its flexibility in multi-modal interaction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates notable novelty through its innovative integration of several elements. Firstly, unifying referring and grounding tasks within a visual prompting framework for RS imagery is a novel contribution. While individual visual prompting techniques exist, their application to multi-source RS data with the stated flexibility is not readily available in existing literature. Furthermore, The multi-modal content integration method enhances feature interplay, combined with a noise augmentation method, appears effective in promoting a robust multi-grained understanding of complex RS images. The multi-source M-RSVP dataset is also a valuable resource for the community. However, the core LLM architecture is based on existing LLMs (Llama2-13B), and components like image encoders (DINOv2, CLIP) and the SAM-based pixel decoder are established elements. The one-stage training approach also follows known principles.

* **Significance:**  The significance lies in addressing limitations of current RS MLLMs, which often lack spatial awareness, flexibility, and comprehensive understanding across different data sources. EarthGPT-X represents a step towards more versatile and practical RS applications. The ability to interact flexibly with RS imagery, focusing on specific regions with varied prompts, has clear value for tasks like disaster response, environmental monitoring, and urban planning.  The performance gains reported compared to existing SOTA MLLMs validate the effectiveness of the proposed architecture and training approach. The paper highlights a clear need within the remote sensing domain, but also notes its general applicability to other domains.

* **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive Architecture: The architecture seamlessly integrates various components, effectively unifying referring and grounding tasks.
    *   Effective Training Strategy: The one-stage training approach simplifies training and promotes cross-domain knowledge transfer.
    *   Extensive Evaluation: Qualitative and quantitative experiments showcase the model's superior performance and versatility across diverse tasks.
    *   New Dataset: Introduction of the M-RSVP dataset addresses the need for visually prompted datasets in remote sensing.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * Reliance on Existing Models: EarthGPT-X relies on well-established LLMs and image encoders which limits its independent novelty.
    * Computational resources: Implementation of the proposed training strategies for larger scale MLLMs demands extensive computational resource.
    * Limited SAR/IR Datasets: The paper acknowledges the limitations of existing SAR and infrared datasets for developing visual reasoning capabilities, which might affect the performance on those data sources. While the paper attempts to mitigate this through cross-domain training, expanding these datasets would strengthen the model's capabilities.
    *  Lack of Comparative Architectures: It's not clear why other prompt engineering strategies and/or other foundation models were not considered.
    * No clear implementation documentation.

* **Potential Influence:** EarthGPT-X has the potential to influence future research in RS MLLMs by demonstrating the effectiveness of visual prompting for spatial reasoning. The unified framework and the M-RSVP dataset will likely spur further development in this direction. The focus on flexible interaction is also likely to become a key consideration in future RS MLLM designs.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of remote sensing by addressing the limitations of current MLLMs. The integration of visual prompting, a unified framework for referring and grounding, and the introduction of the M-RSVP dataset demonstrate significant advancements in model design and capability. The extensive evaluation is also a strong point.

However, the reliance on existing pre-trained models (particularly Llama2-13B, SAM encoder/decoder), and the incremental improvements in the training procedure do temper the originality of the work. While the application and integration are novel and significant for the remote sensing community, the underlying technology builds upon prior work.  The reliance on substantial computational resources to effectively train and deploy MLLMs could impede its wider adoption in some areas. Additionally, the paper's influence will be tempered until the model and dataset become more widely accessible and are adopted by the research community. Given these strengths and weaknesses, the work merits a score of 7, reflecting a significant but not revolutionary contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Assesing LLMs in Art Contexts: Critique Generation and Theory of Mind Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12805v1)
- **Authors**: Takaya Arita, Wenxian Zheng, Reiji Suzuki, Fuminori Akiba
- **Abstract**: This study explored how large language models (LLMs) perform in two areas related to art: writing critiques of artworks and reasoning about mental states (Theory of Mind, or ToM) in art-related situations. For the critique generation part, we built a system that combines Noel Carroll's evaluative framework with a broad selection of art criticism theories. The model was prompted to first write a full-length critique and then shorter, more coherent versions using a step-by-step prompting process. These AI-generated critiques were then compared with those written by human experts in a Turing test-style evaluation. In many cases, human subjects had difficulty telling which was which, and the results suggest that LLMs can produce critiques that are not only plausible in style but also rich in interpretation, as long as they are carefully guided. In the second part, we introduced new simple ToM tasks based on situations involving interpretation, emotion, and moral tension, which can appear in the context of art. These go beyond standard false-belief tests and allow for more complex, socially embedded forms of reasoning. We tested 41 recent LLMs and found that their performance varied across tasks and models. In particular, tasks that involved affective or ambiguous situations tended to reveal clearer differences. Taken together, these results help clarify how LLMs respond to complex interpretative challenges, revealing both their cognitive limitations and potential. While our findings do not directly contradict the so-called Generative AI Paradox--the idea that LLMs can produce expert-like output without genuine understanding--they suggest that, depending on how LLMs are instructed, such as through carefully designed prompts, these models may begin to show behaviors that resemble understanding more closely than we might assume.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Assessing LLMs in Art Contexts: Critique Generation and Theory of Mind Evaluation":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in two art-related tasks: generating art critiques and assessing Theory of Mind (ToM) in art contexts.  For critique generation, the authors developed a system (Composer) that combines Noel Carroll's framework for art criticism with various art criticism theories, generating critiques of varying lengths. These AI-generated critiques were then compared against human-written critiques in a Turing test-style evaluation. For the ToM evaluation, the authors introduced new simple ToM tasks related to interpretation, emotion, and moral tension in art, testing 41 recent LLMs.  The study found that LLMs can produce plausible and rich art critiques with careful prompting, and that their ToM performance varies across tasks and models, with affective or ambiguous situations highlighting differences.  The authors suggest that carefully instructed LLMs can exhibit behaviors that more closely resemble understanding than previously assumed, potentially challenging the Generative AI Paradox.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel Application Area:**  The paper applies LLMs to a relatively unexplored area: art criticism and ToM within art.  This is a refreshing departure from more common LLM applications.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The critique generation system is based on a clear theoretical framework (Carroll's) and the prompt engineering is described in detail. The Turing test is also well-structured with clear rules for fair evaluation.
    *   **Interesting Empirical Results:** The finding that human subjects struggled to differentiate AI-generated critiques from human ones is significant, suggesting that LLMs can achieve a high level of stylistic and interpretative competence.  The variations in ToM performance across different models and tasks provides useful insights into LLM limitations.
    *   **Focus on Higher-Order ToM:** The paper attempts to move beyond simple false-belief tests to explore more complex, socially embedded forms of reasoning. This direction is essential for assessing the potential of LLMs in creative fields.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of ToM Tasks:** While the ToM tasks are a step in the right direction, they remain relatively simple and abstract. The fact that are evaluated using a forced choice binary answer, severely limits the types of assessments they can conduct, and their ability to model complex social interactions that truly challenge ToM capabilities.
    *   **Subjectivity in Turing Test Evaluation:** Despite the authors' efforts to control for biases, the Turing test is inherently subjective, and the success of an AI critique might depend on factors beyond the actual quality of the interpretation (e.g., the evaluator's preconceptions, their familiarity with the artwork, etc.). The small sample size of only 60 subjects and five artworks further limits the generalizability of the Turing Test results.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The paper would benefit from ablation studies that dissect the individual contributions of various elements in the system architecture (external knowledge, chain-of-thought prompting, SemArt examples) to the performance of the LLM.
    *   **Unclear Connection between Critique Generation and ToM:**  The paper posits that ToM is relevant to art criticism, but the connection isn't explicitly demonstrated.  It would be more impactful to show how better ToM capabilities actually *improve* the generated critiques.
    *   **Overstatement of Impact:**  The authors' claim that the findings "challenge the Generative AI Paradox" seems overstated. While the paper suggests that carefully instructed LLMs can *resemble* understanding more closely, it does not provide conclusive evidence that LLMs have genuine understanding.

*   **Novelty and Significance:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of LLMs to the art domain and its exploration of ToM in this context. It contributes to the growing body of research evaluating LLM capabilities and limitations. However, the ToM tasks, while innovative, are a bit limited in scope. The significance is moderate. The paper provides some interesting findings and directions for future research, but does not offer groundbreaking discoveries.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    The paper has a number of strengths, including novel application and a generally sound methodology. However, the somewhat simplistic nature of the ToM tasks, the subjective aspects of the Turing test, and lack of clearer connection between ToM and critique quality hold it back.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction for Effective Invisible Watermark Removal
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12809v1)
- **Authors**: Inzamamul Alam, Md Tanvir Islam, Simon S. Woo
- **Abstract**: As digital content becomes increasingly ubiquitous, the need for robust watermark removal techniques has grown due to the inadequacy of existing embedding techniques, which lack robustness. This paper introduces a novel Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction (SADRE) framework for watermark elimination on the web, combining adaptive noise injection, region-specific perturbations, and advanced diffusion-based reconstruction. SADRE disrupts embedded watermarks by injecting targeted noise into latent representations guided by saliency masks although preserving essential image features. A reverse diffusion process ensures high-fidelity image restoration, leveraging adaptive noise levels determined by watermark strength. Our framework is theoretically grounded with stability guarantees and achieves robust watermark removal across diverse scenarios. Empirical evaluations on state-of-the-art (SOTA) watermarking techniques demonstrate SADRE's superiority in balancing watermark disruption and image quality. SADRE sets a new benchmark for watermark elimination, offering a flexible and reliable solution for real-world web content. Code is available on~\href{https://github.com/inzamamulDU/SADRE}{\textbf{https://github.com/inzamamulDU/SADRE}}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SADRE (Saliency-Aware Diffusion Reconstruction), a novel framework for removing invisible watermarks from digital images.  SADRE combines adaptive noise injection guided by saliency masks, region-specific perturbations, and diffusion-based reconstruction. It aims to disrupt embedded watermarks while preserving essential image features and ensuring high-fidelity image restoration.  The noise injection is tailored based on the estimated watermark strength.  A reverse diffusion process reconstructs the image, leveraging adaptive noise levels. The paper argues for the framework's theoretical grounding in Hölder continuity and stability guarantees. Experimental results demonstrate SADRE's superiority in balancing watermark disruption and image quality compared to state-of-the-art watermarking and watermark removal techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of saliency-aware noise injection with diffusion-based reconstruction for watermark removal constitutes a key element of novelty. Using saliency masks to target noise injection provides a smart approach over indiscriminate perturbation. However, the individual components (saliency detection, noise injection, and diffusion models) are not entirely novel in themselves. The key novelty lies in their specific integration within the watermark removal context. The adaptive noise levels based on estimated watermark strength contribute to a more refined approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical need in digital content security: robust watermark removal techniques due to the weakness of existing embedding techniques. The potential for adversarial manipulation of watermarks has created a pressing demand for methods that minimize collateral damage while effectively disrupting the watermark. SADRE seems to achieve a better balance between removal effectiveness and image fidelity, which is a significant advantage over existing methods that often prioritize one at the expense of the other. The real-world applicability of the method, demonstrated by performance against different watermarking strategies, boosts its importance. The theoretical justifications provide a sound foundation that could inspire future work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework design is well-motivated, addressing the limitations of existing watermark removal approaches.
    *   The integration of adaptive noise injection with diffusion-based reconstruction offers a more nuanced approach to watermark disruption.
    *   The use of saliency masks targets regions of influence of watermarks, therefore preserving the image quality
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluations demonstrate superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods.
    *   The paper offers some theoretical justification.
    *   The code availability improves the reproducibility of the paper

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper makes claims regarding theoretical guarantees (Hölder continuity), but may need more in-depth analysis and proofs to be more convincing.
    *   The novelty, while present, could be increased by, for instance, adaptively learning the noise distributions rather than relying on predetermined distributions.
    *   The paper acknowledges prior art by referencing the SOTA watermarking techniques, but may want to include a section that more specifically distinguishes this work from related watermark *removal* research.
    *   The limitations of the proposed technique, such as its computational cost or potential sensitivity to specific types of images or watermarks, need to be discussed.

*   **Potential Influence:** SADRE has the potential to influence future research in watermark removal by demonstrating the effectiveness of saliency-aware noise injection and diffusion models. It could inspire the development of more robust and sophisticated watermark removal techniques. Also, it serves as a good reference against which to compare novel watermarking methods.

* **Clarity**: The paper can improve its clarity to ensure the ease of accessibility for the readers.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a reasonably novel and significant contribution to the field of watermark removal. The proposed SADRE framework offers a more balanced and effective approach compared to existing techniques. The integration of saliency awareness and diffusion models is a promising avenue for future research. While individual components aren't entirely new, their combination and tailored application within this context are innovative.
The limitations, mainly the theoretical analysis and level of novelty relative to the components used, prevent a higher score.  A more in-depth theoretical underpinning and a more nuanced justification for the choices made in the framework would strengthen the paper significantly. The strong experimental results, however, demonstrate the practical value of SADRE, justifying a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Image-Editing Specialists: An RLAIF Approach for Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12833v1)
- **Authors**: Elior Benarous, Yilun Du, Heng Yang
- **Abstract**: We present a novel approach to training specialized instruction-based image-editing diffusion models, addressing key challenges in structural preservation with input images and semantic alignment with user prompts. We introduce an online reinforcement learning framework that aligns the diffusion model with human preferences without relying on extensive human annotations or curating a large dataset. Our method significantly improves the realism and alignment with instructions in two ways. First, the proposed models achieve precise and structurally coherent modifications in complex scenes while maintaining high fidelity in instruction-irrelevant areas. Second, they capture fine nuances in the desired edit by leveraging a visual prompt, enabling detailed control over visual edits without lengthy textual prompts. This approach simplifies users' efforts to achieve highly specific edits, requiring only 5 reference images depicting a certain concept for training. Experimental results demonstrate that our models can perform intricate edits in complex scenes, after just 10 training steps. Finally, we showcase the versatility of our method by applying it to robotics, where enhancing the visual realism of simulated environments through targeted sim-to-real image edits improves their utility as proxies for real-world settings.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the provided paper and here's a summary and critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new approach for training instruction-based image editing diffusion models. The core contribution is an online reinforcement learning framework using AI feedback (RLAIF) to address challenges related to structural preservation within the input image and semantic alignment with user prompts. Instead of relying on large datasets of human annotations or denoising objectives, the approach aligns the diffusion model with AI-generated preferences in the latent space of an encoder. The method aims to achieve precise and structurally coherent modifications in complex scenes, maintaining high fidelity in instruction-irrelevant regions. It leverages visual prompts (a small set of reference images) to capture fine nuances in desired edits. The paper demonstrates the method's effectiveness with intricate edits in complex scenes after a limited number of training steps. Finally, it explores the utility of the approach in robotics by enhancing the realism of simulated environments through sim-to-real image edits.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by incorporating RLAIF for image editing. The use of AI-generated feedback to guide the diffusion model instead of relying on human annotations or the denoising objective is a significant departure from common practices. The combination of textual instructions and visual prompts for enhanced control is also a valuable contribution. The application of the method to sim-to-real transfer in robotics is innovative and practical.

*   **Significance:** The paper's contribution lies in improving the precision and control in instruction-based image editing. The ability to perform targeted edits while preserving structural integrity is crucial for various applications, including content creation, data augmentation, and robotics. The RLAIF approach offers a more scalable and potentially less biased alternative to human-annotated datasets for training diffusion models. The sim-to-real application highlights the potential of the method for accelerating robotics research by improving the realism of simulated environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The RLAIF approach reduces reliance on expensive and potentially inconsistent human annotations.
    *   The use of visual prompts alongside textual instructions enables fine-grained control over edits.
    *   The method demonstrates strong performance in structural preservation and semantic alignment.
    *   The sim-to-real application provides practical evidence of the method's utility.
    *   The paper is well-written and presents a clear explanation of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper's reliance on AI-generated feedback introduces potential biases from the AI models used for providing feedback, although they claim to mitigate these issues with their design.
    *   The dependence on pre-trained models, such as InstructPix2Pix and the depth estimation model, can limit the overall performance and generalizability of the approach.
    *   The quantitative evaluation could be more comprehensive, potentially including user studies to assess the perceived realism and quality of the edits.
    *   The improvements in some metrics are marginal and could be attributed to the specific datasets/benchmarks employed.
    *   The approach is primarly focused on texture changes and surface modifications, limiting its applicability to scenarios with substantial structural changes.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by promoting the use of RLAIF for training diffusion models. The combination of visual prompts and textual instructions offers a promising direction for achieving more precise and controllable image editing. The application to sim-to-real transfer in robotics can encourage further research on using image editing techniques to bridge the gap between simulation and reality.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The primary reason for the "middle" rating is the reliance on already-existing models (InstructPix2Pix, DINOv2 etc) and the limited scope of the applications. The technical novelty is fairly high, but the overall contribution remains limited by already existing architectures. There are also concerns that the AI-generated feedback is essentially a complicated form of regularization. More compelling evidence is needed to firmly establish the benefits of visual prompt editing and AI feedback over existing image editing methods. However, the idea to use AI feedback is solid and can prove more useful as technology advances.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### DashChat: Interactive Authoring of Industrial Dashboard Design Prototypes through Conversation with LLM-Powered Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12865v1)
- **Authors**: S. Shen, Z. Lin, W. Liu, C. Xin, W. Dai, S. Chen, X. Wen, X. Lan
- **Abstract**: Industrial dashboards, commonly deployed by organizations such as enterprises and governments, are increasingly crucial in data communication and decision-making support across various domains. Designing an industrial dashboard prototype is particularly challenging due to its visual complexity, which can include data visualization, layout configuration, embellishments, and animations. Additionally, in real-world industrial settings, designers often encounter numerous constraints. For instance, when companies negotiate collaborations with clients and determine design plans, they typically need to demo design prototypes and iterate on them based on mock data quickly. Such a task is very common and crucial during the ideation stage, as it not only helps save developmental costs but also avoids data-related issues such as lengthy data handover periods. However, existing authoring tools of dashboards are mostly not tailored to such prototyping needs, and motivated by these gaps, we propose DashChat, an interactive system that leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate industrial dashboard design prototypes from natural language. We collaborated closely with designers from the industry and derived the requirements based on their practical experience. First, by analyzing 114 high-quality industrial dashboards, we summarized their common design patterns and inject the identified ones into LLMs as reference. Next, we built a multi-agent pipeline powered by LLMs to understand textual requirements from users and generate practical, aesthetic prototypes. Besides, functionally distinct, parallel-operating agents are created to enable efficient generation. Then, we developed a user-friendly interface that supports text-based interaction for generating and modifying prototypes. Two user studies demonstrated that our system is both effective and efficient in supporting design prototyping.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "DashChat," an interactive system that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to assist designers in creating industrial dashboard prototypes from natural language descriptions. Recognizing the challenges of dashboard design (visual complexity, diverse requirements, rapid prototyping needs without real data), DashChat employs a multi-agent LLM pipeline to generate aesthetic prototypes.  The system incorporates a design space derived from an analysis of 114 existing industrial dashboards, injecting these patterns into LLMs for reference. The system is implemented with a user-friendly interface consisting of a Chat Panel and a History Panel to enable iterative refinement and reviewing of specifications. User studies demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of DashChat in supporting design prototyping.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper’s primary novelty lies in combining LLMs, a comprehensive industrial dashboard design space (derived from real-world examples), and an interactive system to address the practical need for rapid dashboard prototyping *before* real data is available. While individual components (LLM-based design tools, dashboard design patterns, interactive interfaces) exist, their integration within the specific context of *industrial dashboard prototyping* distinguishes this work. The multi-agent architecture is also a notable contribution, contributing to efficiency by parallelizing the task.
*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant because industrial dashboards are crucial for data communication and decision-making within organizations. Rapid prototyping without real data access is a common and critical need in the industry. Addressing this bottleneck with an LLM-powered tool has the potential to enhance design workflows, reduce time to market, and lower development costs.  The paper's potential impact is strengthened by the authors' close collaboration with industry designers at Alibaba Cloud's DataV, showcasing a direct link to real-world needs.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates a relevant and practical problem in the dashboard design process.
    *   **Comprehensive approach:** The system addresses the problem with a well-designed multi-agent architecture, incorporating knowledge from real-world dashboards.
    *   **User-friendly interface:** The chat-based interface and history panel make the system accessible and easy to use for designers.
    *   **Evaluation:** The user studies provide evidence of the system's effectiveness and efficiency.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on LLMs:** The system's performance is intrinsically linked to the capabilities (and limitations) of the underlying LLMs, particularly Qwen2.5-Max. Future improvements in LLMs would automatically benefit the system, but current limitations in creativity and logical rigor (as discussed in the paper) remain.
    *   **Generalizability:** The design patterns were extracted from dashboards created by DataV. Although DataV is a leading company in the field, future work should analyze whether the extracted patterns are relevant in other dashboard design environments.
    *   **Data Simulation improvement:** Data simulation isn’t currently fully integrated. The ability could be enhanced by integrating external knowledge sources.
    *   **Stylization limitations:** Focus on a global palette recommendation and embellishment generation, centering on a color-related design style.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper is a strong contribution (score 7) because it effectively solves a practical problem in a relevant domain using a novel integration of existing technologies (LLMs, design patterns, interactive interfaces).  The significance of the problem, along with the user study results, solidifies the paper's impact. While it suffers from limitations related to LLM dependability, and the limited scope of design pattern analysis, these can be addressed in future work. The paper sets a clear direction for future research on LLM-based design tools in the context of industrial data visualization.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### EmoVoice: LLM-based Emotional Text-To-Speech Model with Freestyle Text Prompting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12867v1)
- **Authors**: Guanrou Yang, Chen Yang, Qian Chen, Ziyang Ma, Wenxi Chen, Wen Wang, Tianrui Wang, Yifan Yang, Zhikang Niu, Wenrui Liu, Fan Yu, Zhihao Du, Zhifu Gao, ShiLiang Zhang, Xie Chen
- **Abstract**: Human speech goes beyond the mere transfer of information; it is a profound exchange of emotions and a connection between individuals. While Text-to-Speech (TTS) models have made huge progress, they still face challenges in controlling the emotional expression in the generated speech. In this work, we propose EmoVoice, a novel emotion-controllable TTS model that exploits large language models (LLMs) to enable fine-grained freestyle natural language emotion control, and a phoneme boost variant design that makes the model output phoneme tokens and audio tokens in parallel to enhance content consistency, inspired by chain-of-thought (CoT) and modality-of-thought (CoM) techniques. Besides, we introduce EmoVoice-DB, a high-quality 40-hour English emotion dataset featuring expressive speech and fine-grained emotion labels with natural language descriptions. EmoVoice achieves state-of-the-art performance on the English EmoVoice-DB test set using only synthetic training data, and on the Chinese Secap test set using our in-house data. We further investigate the reliability of existing emotion evaluation metrics and their alignment with human perceptual preferences, and explore using SOTA multimodal LLMs GPT-4o-audio and Gemini to assess emotional speech. Demo samples are available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/EmoVoice-DF55. Dataset, code, and checkpoints will be released.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EmoVoice, a novel emotion-controllable text-to-speech (TTS) model leveraging large language models (LLMs) for fine-grained emotion control via freestyle text prompting.  EmoVoice uses Qwen2.5-0.5B as the backbone LLM to comprehend emotional instructions and incorporates a phoneme boost variant (EmoVoice-PP) that outputs phoneme and audio tokens in parallel to enhance content consistency.  The authors also present EmoVoice-DB, a new high-quality 40-hour English emotion dataset with natural language emotion descriptions. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on English and Chinese test sets, utilizing synthetic training data.  The authors further analyze existing emotion evaluation metrics and explore using multimodal LLMs to assess emotional speech.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses an important and challenging problem in TTS: achieving fine-grained control over emotional expression. Leveraging LLMs for this purpose is a logical and promising direction. The key strengths of the paper lie in:

*   **Novel Model Architecture:** The EmoVoice architecture, especially the EmoVoice-PP variant incorporating parallel phoneme prediction, represents a tangible contribution. The phoneme boost idea, inspired by CoT and CoM, is well-motivated.
*   **High-Quality Dataset:** The creation of EmoVoice-DB fills a significant gap in the availability of suitable training data for emotion-controllable TTS. The emphasis on natural language descriptions and the careful filtering process enhance its value. However, the reliance on GPT-4o for dataset generation raises concerns about potential biases or limitations inherited from the source model.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a detailed evaluation across multiple metrics (WER, emotion similarity, recall, UTMOS) and datasets. The subjective evaluation with human raters provides valuable insights. The exploration of using multimodal LLMs for emotion assessment is a novel and worthwhile endeavor, although the results indicate limitations.
*   **State-of-the-Art Performance:** Demonstrating SOTA performance on both English and Chinese datasets is a significant achievement.

However, some weaknesses need consideration:

*   **Over-Reliance on Synthetic Data:**  The model is trained primarily on synthetic data generated by GPT-4o. While this allows for precise control over emotion labeling, it raises concerns about the model's ability to generalize to real-world data and potentially inherit biases from the data generation model.  Ablation studies comparing performance with real-world data or transfer learning experiments would strengthen the results. This significantly limits the robustness and real-world applicability of the model.
*   **Limited Novelty in LLM Integration:** The integration of LLMs for TTS is not entirely novel. The paper relies on Qwen2.5-0.5B, which is not a cutting-edge LLM, and adapting an LLM for text-to-speech has been done before. The key novelty lies in the specific architecture and the use of the phoneme boost technique.
*   **Evaluation Metric Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of existing emotion evaluation metrics. However, the reliance on emotion2vec, which is also limited, weakens the claims regarding emotion control. While the exploration of LLM-based evaluation is promising, the results indicate that current LLMs are not yet reliable for this task.
