- **Score**: 7/10

### SkyReels-V2: Infinite-length Film Generative Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13074v1)
- **Authors**: Guibin Chen, Dixuan Lin, Jiangping Yang, Chunze Lin, Juncheng Zhu, Mingyuan Fan, Hao Zhang, Sheng Chen, Zheng Chen, Chengchen Ma, Weiming Xiong, Wei Wang, Nuo Pang, Kang Kang, Zhiheng Xu, Yuzhe Jin, Yupeng Liang, Yubing Song, Peng Zhao, Boyuan Xu, Di Qiu, Debang Li, Zhengcong Fei, Yang Li, Yahui Zhou
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video generation have been driven by diffusion models and autoregressive frameworks, yet critical challenges persist in harmonizing prompt adherence, visual quality, motion dynamics, and duration: compromises in motion dynamics to enhance temporal visual quality, constrained video duration (5-10 seconds) to prioritize resolution, and inadequate shot-aware generation stemming from general-purpose MLLMs' inability to interpret cinematic grammar, such as shot composition, actor expressions, and camera motions. These intertwined limitations hinder realistic long-form synthesis and professional film-style generation. To address these limitations, we propose SkyReels-V2, an Infinite-length Film Generative Model, that synergizes Multi-modal Large Language Model (MLLM), Multi-stage Pretraining, Reinforcement Learning, and Diffusion Forcing Framework. Firstly, we design a comprehensive structural representation of video that combines the general descriptions by the Multi-modal LLM and the detailed shot language by sub-expert models. Aided with human annotation, we then train a unified Video Captioner, named SkyCaptioner-V1, to efficiently label the video data. Secondly, we establish progressive-resolution pretraining for the fundamental video generation, followed by a four-stage post-training enhancement: Initial concept-balanced Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) improves baseline quality; Motion-specific Reinforcement Learning (RL) training with human-annotated and synthetic distortion data addresses dynamic artifacts; Our diffusion forcing framework with non-decreasing noise schedules enables long-video synthesis in an efficient search space; Final high-quality SFT refines visual fidelity. All the code and models are available at https://github.com/SkyworkAI/SkyReels-V2.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SkyReels-V2 paper:

**Summary:**

The SkyReels-V2 paper introduces a new approach for infinite-length film generation, addressing key challenges in video generation such as prompt adherence, visual quality, motion dynamics, and duration limitations. The model combines a multi-modal large language model (MLLM), multi-stage pretraining, reinforcement learning, and a diffusion forcing framework. SkyReels-V2 first uses a comprehensive video representation combining MLLM and sub-expert models for detailed shot language understanding. A video captioner is trained for efficient data labeling. Then, progressive-resolution pretraining is performed, followed by a four-stage post-training enhancement: supervised fine-tuning (SFT), motion-specific reinforcement learning (RL), diffusion forcing, and final high-quality SFT. The diffusion forcing framework, which utilizes non-decreasing noise schedules, enables long-video synthesis. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in prompt adherence, motion quality, and film-style long-video generation, enabling applications like story generation, image-to-video synthesis, camera director, and elements-to-video generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper proposes a holistic framework that combines various techniques effectively. The synergy between MLLMs, RL, and diffusion models addresses multiple challenges in video generation.
    *   **Long-Duration Video Generation:** The diffusion forcing framework is a significant contribution as it enables the generation of videos with virtually unlimited length, addressing a major limitation of previous methods.
    *   **Detailed Shot Language Understanding:** The use of specialized sub-expert models to enhance the shot language understanding helps generate more cinematic and professional-looking videos, improving prompt adherence.
    *   **Motion Quality Improvement:**  The RL component, coupled with a semi-automatic preference data collection pipeline, targets a critical aspect of video generation: realistic and dynamic motion.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper demonstrates superior performance compared to existing open-source methods on benchmarks and human evaluations.
    *   **Open-Source Release:** The authors open-source both code and models, fostering community research and facilitating advancements in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The proposed pipeline is intricate, with multiple stages and components. This complexity may make it challenging for other researchers to reproduce or build upon. The paper should provide a more detailed analysis of the contribution of each part of the framework.
    *   **Error Accumulation with Diffusion Forcing:** The paper acknowledges error accumulation in extended generations due to the diffusion forcing framework but only addresses it with a simple stabilization technique. This remains a potential limitation for practical applications.
    *   **Scalability of Data Annotation:**  While the video captioner is a strong component, the cost and scalability of collecting human-annotated data for motion quality preference and expert knowledge remains a concern for future developments.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the overall framework is novel, some of the individual components (e.g., DPO, architecture of diffusion model) are based on well-established techniques. The paper's value lies more in the effective integration and application of these methods.
    *   **Reliance on Specific MLLMs:** The reliance on Qwen2.5-VL-32B potentially locks the future advancement of the work into the development of that specific MLLM. A future design that allow for a more plug-and-play capability to facilitate the model to be used together with newer and better MLLM model could potentially increase the paper's value and significance.
    *   **Limited ablation study** The paper would benefit from a detailed ablation study to fully elucidate the impact of each contribution towards the performance lift, to avoid the "kitchen-sink" approach.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The SkyReels-V2 model is a significant step towards generating realistic, high-quality, and long-form videos.
    *   The integrated approach and specific solutions for prompt adherence, motion dynamics, and long-duration generation represent valuable contributions to the field.
    *   The open-source release will have a positive impact on the community, enabling further research and development in video generation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a compelling and well-executed approach to address several key challenges in video generation, particularly regarding long-duration synthesis and cinematic control. The results demonstrate strong performance on benchmarks and human evaluations. However, the complexity of the system, dependence on specific MLLMs, and potential error accumulation in long-form generation hold back the score from being higher. Furthermore, more comprehensive ablation study would contribute to the paper and potentially improve the score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### EventVAD: Training-Free Event-Aware Video Anomaly Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13092v1)
- **Authors**: Yihua Shao, Haojin He, Sijie Li, Siyu Chen, Xinwei Long, Fanhu Zeng, Yuxuan Fan, Muyang Zhang, Ziyang Yan, Ao Ma, Xiaochen Wang, Hao Tang, Yan Wang, Shuyan Li
- **Abstract**: Video Anomaly Detection~(VAD) focuses on identifying anomalies within videos. Supervised methods require an amount of in-domain training data and often struggle to generalize to unseen anomalies. In contrast, training-free methods leverage the intrinsic world knowledge of large language models (LLMs) to detect anomalies but face challenges in localizing fine-grained visual transitions and diverse events. Therefore, we propose EventVAD, an event-aware video anomaly detection framework that combines tailored dynamic graph architectures and multimodal LLMs through temporal-event reasoning. Specifically, EventVAD first employs dynamic spatiotemporal graph modeling with time-decay constraints to capture event-aware video features. Then, it performs adaptive noise filtering and uses signal ratio thresholding to detect event boundaries via unsupervised statistical features. The statistical boundary detection module reduces the complexity of processing long videos for MLLMs and improves their temporal reasoning through event consistency. Finally, it utilizes a hierarchical prompting strategy to guide MLLMs in performing reasoning before determining final decisions. We conducted extensive experiments on the UCF-Crime and XD-Violence datasets. The results demonstrate that EventVAD with a 7B MLLM achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) in training-free settings, outperforming strong baselines that use 7B or larger MLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EventVAD: Training-Free Event-Aware Video Anomaly Detection":

**Summary**

The paper presents EventVAD, a novel training-free framework for video anomaly detection.  It addresses the limitations of existing training-free methods, which struggle with fine-grained localization and diverse events, by combining dynamic graph architectures and multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for event-aware reasoning. The framework employs a dynamic spatiotemporal graph with time-decay constraints to capture event-aware features, adaptive noise filtering, and signal ratio thresholding for event boundary detection. This reduces the complexity of processing long videos for MLLMs and improves their temporal reasoning. A hierarchical prompting strategy guides MLLMs in decision-making. Experimental results on UCF-Crime and XD-Violence datasets demonstrate that EventVAD achieves state-of-the-art performance in training-free settings, even outperforming baselines with larger MLLMs, while significantly reducing model parameters.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the architecture integrating dynamic graph representation with an MLLM framework. The use of a time-decaying spatiotemporal graph to capture event-aware features is interesting and seems effective for event boundary detection. While MLLMs have been explored for VAD before (e.g., LAVAD), EventVAD's novel combination of graph-based feature extraction with event-centric prompting represents a step forward. The adaptive noise filtering and boundary detection using statistical features are also important additions.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the improved performance in a training-free setting. The ability to detect anomalies without extensive labeled data is highly desirable, as labeled video data is expensive and time-consuming to obtain. The reduction in model parameters from 13B to 7B while maintaining or improving performance is a practical advantage, reducing computational and storage costs. The approach is important given the move toward large foundation models in computer vision.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong empirical results on standard benchmark datasets.  The improvements over existing training-free and even some weakly-supervised methods are compelling.
    *   Clear description of the architecture and its components.
    *   Ablation studies effectively highlight the contribution of each component.
    *   The qualitative analysis provides insights into how the model works and where it improves over existing methods.
    *   The authors have clearly identified the limitations of existing approaches and addressed them with a well-designed framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on RAFT optical flow might limit the real-time applicability of the method, as computing optical flow can be computationally expensive. However, the authors addressed this by reducing the model parameters, so the framework would still be less expensive to use than current approaches.
    *   The hyperparameter selection for time decay and semantic-motion fusion relies on empirical tuning.  A more principled approach to parameter selection could be beneficial.
    *   While the method shows strong performance on UCF-Crime and XD-Violence, the generalization ability to other types of datasets and anomalies could be further explored.
    *   The approach is training-free but relies on pre-trained models (CLIP, RAFT, MLLM). This means the performance is highly dependent on the quality of those pre-trained models and biases they might contain.

*   **Potential Impact:** EventVAD has the potential to influence the design of future VAD systems by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining graph-based feature extraction with MLLMs for event-aware reasoning. It can inspire further research into training-free approaches that leverage the world knowledge encoded in large pre-trained models. Furthermore, its potential for lower computational overhead makes it useful in practical applications. The framework also has broader applicability, and could be adapted to other video understanding tasks such as video captioning and video question answering.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper presents a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated framework for training-free video anomaly detection. The novelty in combining graph attention with MLLMs for event-aware reasoning and the demonstrated performance improvements justify the claim that the method advances the field. Although it has some weaknesses related to the computational cost of RAFT and dependence on pre-trained model biases, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses.

### RF-DETR Object Detection vs YOLOv12 : A Study of Transformer-based and CNN-based Architectures for Single-Class and Multi-Class Greenfruit Detection in Complex Orchard Environments Under Label Ambiguity
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13099v1)
- **Authors**: Ranjan Sapkota, Rahul Harsha Cheppally, Ajay Sharda, Manoj Karkee
- **Abstract**: This study conducts a detailed comparison of RF-DETR object detection base model and YOLOv12 object detection model configurations for detecting greenfruits in a complex orchard environment marked by label ambiguity, occlusions, and background blending. A custom dataset was developed featuring both single-class (greenfruit) and multi-class (occluded and non-occluded greenfruits) annotations to assess model performance under dynamic real-world conditions. RF-DETR object detection model, utilizing a DINOv2 backbone and deformable attention, excelled in global context modeling, effectively identifying partially occluded or ambiguous greenfruits. In contrast, YOLOv12 leveraged CNN-based attention for enhanced local feature extraction, optimizing it for computational efficiency and edge deployment. RF-DETR achieved the highest mean Average Precision (mAP50) of 0.9464 in single-class detection, proving its superior ability to localize greenfruits in cluttered scenes. Although YOLOv12N recorded the highest mAP@50:95 of 0.7620, RF-DETR consistently outperformed in complex spatial scenarios. For multi-class detection, RF-DETR led with an mAP@50 of 0.8298, showing its capability to differentiate between occluded and non-occluded fruits, while YOLOv12L scored highest in mAP@50:95 with 0.6622, indicating better classification in detailed occlusion contexts. Training dynamics analysis highlighted RF-DETR's swift convergence, particularly in single-class settings where it plateaued within 10 epochs, demonstrating the efficiency of transformer-based architectures in adapting to dynamic visual data. These findings validate RF-DETR's effectiveness for precision agricultural applications, with YOLOv12 suited for fast-response scenarios. >Index Terms: RF-DETR object detection, YOLOv12, YOLOv13, YOLOv14, YOLOv15, YOLOE, YOLO World, YOLO, You Only Look Once, Roboflow, Detection Transformers, CNNs
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper presents a comparative study of two object detection models, RF-DETR (a Transformer-based model) and YOLOv12 (a CNN-based model), for greenfruit detection in complex orchard environments. It investigates the models' performance in both single-class (all greenfruits as one class) and multi-class (occluded vs. non-occluded greenfruits) detection scenarios. The study uses a custom dataset of orchard images with corresponding annotations. The results show that RF-DETR generally outperforms YOLOv12 in accuracy, especially in handling occlusions and camouflage, although YOLOv12 models reach a comparatively high performance at the more stringent AP@50:95 mAP metric in many cases. Furthermore, RF-DETR is shown to converge faster during training. The authors conclude that RF-DETR is suitable for accuracy-critical agricultural tasks, while YOLOv12 remains ideal for speed-sensitive deployments.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant and Timely Research Question:** The paper addresses a relevant problem in precision agriculture: accurate and efficient fruit detection in complex environments. This is crucial for tasks like yield estimation and automated harvesting.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The study employs a sound methodology by using a custom dataset collected from a real-world orchard, standard evaluation metrics, and a fair comparison of the two models using identical training setups. The ablation study and discussion of the performance of each model is good.
    *   **Clear Presentation of Results:** The results are presented clearly using tables and figures, allowing for easy comparison of the models' performance. Qualitative results are also presented, which visually highlights the strengths and weaknesses of each approach.
    *   **Useful Insights:** The paper provides valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of Transformer-based and CNN-based object detection architectures for this specific application. The discussion of label ambiguity and its impact on model performance is particularly insightful.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly organized, making it easy to follow the research process and understand the key findings.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty:** The core idea of comparing YOLO and DETR variants isn't particularly novel *in principle*. Similar comparisons have been made in other contexts. The primary novelty lies in the *application* to this specific agricultural task (greenfruit detection), the custom dataset, and comparison of particularly *recent* models.
    *   **Dataset Size and Diversity:** While the dataset is custom-built and relevant, its size (857 images) could be considered relatively modest. Increasing the size and diversity of the dataset (e.g., different orchards, lighting conditions, fruit varieties) would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Model Variety Within Families:** While the chosen RF-DETR and YOLOv12 models are representatives from their respective architectures, exploring other variants (e.g. the largest RF-DETR model vs different sized YOLOv12 models), with each model type's best hyperparameter settings, would provide a more comprehensive picture.
    *   **Limited Quantitative Runtime Analysis:** Although the authors qualitatively state that RF-DETR is better for accuracy-critical tasks, while YOLO is better for speed-sensitive tasks, there is no comprehensive, quantitative comparison of the runtime of the models. This information would be helpful for practitioners to make informed decisions about which model to deploy.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The paper would benefit from inclusion of detailed ablation studies on each model to determine the impact of specific architectural choices on overall performance. The paper should include justifications for not running additional RF-DETR models with different hyperparameters.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper's significance lies primarily in its practical contribution to precision agriculture. The findings can help researchers and practitioners choose appropriate object detection models for specific applications in orchard management.
    *   The study contributes to the growing body of knowledge on the application of deep learning to agricultural problems.
    *   The paper highlights the importance of addressing label ambiguity in agricultural datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, I am assigning a score of **6.5** to this paper.

**Rationale:** While the paper addresses a relevant problem with a sound methodology and provides useful insights, its novelty is somewhat limited because it's primarily an application of existing models to a specific domain. While the dataset is custom-built, its size and diversity could be improved. The absence of a more extensive and quantitative runtime analysis, a detailed hyperparameter justification, and the lack of more detailed ablation studies for each model weaken the comparative analysis. This keeps the paper from making a substantial groundbreaking contribution to the *computer vision* field itself, even if the empirical results have a meaningful impact on *precision agriculture* researchers. Thus, it contributes positively to the field but is not a major leap forward. The significance of this paper is in demonstrating the suitability of emerging object detection architectures, like RF-DETR, for real-world orchard applications, but it has some shortcomings that hold it back from a higher rating.

### UniEdit-Flow: Unleashing Inversion and Editing in the Era of Flow Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13109v1)
- **Authors**: Guanlong Jiao, Biqing Huang, Kuan-Chieh Wang, Renjie Liao
- **Abstract**: Flow matching models have emerged as a strong alternative to diffusion models, but existing inversion and editing methods designed for diffusion are often ineffective or inapplicable to them. The straight-line, non-crossing trajectories of flow models pose challenges for diffusion-based approaches but also open avenues for novel solutions. In this paper, we introduce a predictor-corrector-based framework for inversion and editing in flow models. First, we propose Uni-Inv, an effective inversion method designed for accurate reconstruction. Building on this, we extend the concept of delayed injection to flow models and introduce Uni-Edit, a region-aware, robust image editing approach. Our methodology is tuning-free, model-agnostic, efficient, and effective, enabling diverse edits while ensuring strong preservation of edit-irrelevant regions. Extensive experiments across various generative models demonstrate the superiority and generalizability of Uni-Inv and Uni-Edit, even under low-cost settings. Project page: https://uniedit-flow.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "UNIEDIT-FLOW: Unleashing Inversion and Editing in the Era of Flow Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UniEdit-Flow, a novel framework for image inversion and editing specifically designed for flow-matching generative models.  It addresses the limitations of existing diffusion-based inversion and editing techniques when applied to flow models like SD3 and Flux. The core contributions are:

1.  **Uni-Inv:** An effective predictor-corrector-based inversion method tailored to flow models. It aims for accurate reconstruction of original images from their latent space representations.
2.  **Uni-Edit:** An image editing approach that incorporates "delayed injection" with region-aware guidance. It leverages the unique straight-line trajectories of flow models to achieve both targeted edits and strong preservation of irrelevant regions.
3.  **Model-Agnostic & Tuning-Free:** The method is designed to be adaptable to various generative model architectures (including diffusion models), and it requires no task-specific training or hyperparameter tuning.

The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of UniEdit-Flow through quantitative and qualitative experiments across different generative models, showcasing its superiority in reconstruction accuracy and editing quality compared to existing methods. The experiments highlight the advantages of the proposed approach even in low-cost settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit adaptation of inversion and editing techniques to the specific characteristics of flow-matching generative models, particularly their deterministic and straight-line trajectories. While diffusion-based editing techniques are well-established, their direct application to flow models has proven challenging. The "delayed injection" and region-aware correction mechanisms are tailored responses to these challenges and the predictor-corrector method of inversion is a novel approach.
*   **Significance:** Flow models have recently gained prominence, particularly in high-resolution image generation and editing. By providing effective inversion and editing capabilities for these models, the paper addresses a critical gap and unlocks new possibilities for leveraging flow models in various applications. The claim of being tuning-free is important, reducing the complexity and expertise required to use the technique.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the issues with applying existing diffusion-based techniques to flow models.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The proposed methods are well-motivated by the characteristics of flow models. The predictor-corrector framework is a reasonable approach to address the issues.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluations across different models and datasets. The ablation studies offer insights into the contribution of different components.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The method is presented as model-agnostic and tuning-free, making it more accessible and practical for real-world use.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Nature:** While the approach is well-engineered for flow models, the individual components (predictor-corrector, delayed injection) are not entirely new concepts. The novelty is primarily in their combination and adaptation to flow-based generation.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:**  While the theoretical analysis offers some guarantee about the local error, a more in-depth theoretical analysis of the convergence and stability of the inversion and editing processes would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Dependence on Mask Quality:** The region-adaptive guidance heavily relies on the mask's quality, which depends on the difference between latents. If the difference is subtle or the model struggles to associate the prompts with edits in specific regions, it could impact the quality.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by enabling more widespread use of flow models in image editing applications. Its model-agnostic nature and the elimination of extensive tuning could make it valuable for researchers and practitioners alike.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to image editing by explicitly targeting and addressing the challenges posed by flow-matching generative models. The proposed methods are well-motivated, thoroughly evaluated, and practically relevant. However, the incremental nature of the individual components and the lack of deeper theoretical analysis, coupled with mask dependence keeps it from a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VistaDPO: Video Hierarchical Spatial-Temporal Direct Preference Optimization for Large Video Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13122v1)
- **Authors**: Haojian Huang, Haodong Chen, Shengqiong Wu, Meng Luo, Jinlan Fu, Xinya Du, Hanwang Zhang, Hao Fei
- **Abstract**: Large Video Models (LVMs) built upon Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promise in video understanding but often suffer from misalignment with human intuition and video hallucination issues. To address these challenges, we introduce VistaDPO, a novel framework for Video Hierarchical Spatial-Temporal Direct Preference Optimization. VistaDPO enhances text-video preference alignment across three hierarchical levels: i) Instance Level, aligning overall video content with responses; ii) Temporal Level, aligning video temporal semantics with event descriptions; and iii) Perceptive Level, aligning spatial objects with language tokens. Given the lack of datasets for fine-grained video-language preference alignment, we construct VistaDPO-7k, a dataset of 7.2K QA pairs annotated with chosen and rejected responses, along with spatial-temporal grounding information such as timestamps, keyframes, and bounding boxes. Extensive experiments on benchmarks such as Video Hallucination, Video QA, and Captioning performance tasks demonstrate that VistaDPO significantly improves the performance of existing LVMs, effectively mitigating video-language misalignment and hallucination. The code and data are available at https://github.com/HaroldChen19/VistaDPO.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a rigorous critical evaluation, including a novelty/significance score.

**Paper Summary:**

The paper introduces VistaDPO, a novel framework for Video Hierarchical Spatial-Temporal Direct Preference Optimization, aimed at improving the performance of Large Video Models (LVMs). The core idea is to enhance text-video alignment by optimizing preferences at three hierarchical levels: (1) Instance Level (overall video content vs. responses), (2) Temporal Level (video temporal semantics vs. event descriptions), and (3) Perceptive Level (spatial objects vs. language tokens). To enable this fine-grained optimization, the authors construct VistaDPO-7k, a new dataset of 7.2K QA pairs with annotations capturing spatial-temporal grounding information like timestamps, keyframes, and bounding boxes. The effectiveness of VistaDPO is demonstrated through experiments on benchmarks like Video Hallucination, Video QA, and Captioning, showing significant improvements over existing LVMs by mitigating video-language misalignment and hallucination.  The code and data are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in its hierarchical approach to video-language preference alignment. While DPO has been applied to other modalities, and even video, VistaDPO is, to the best of my knowledge, the first to explicitly decompose the alignment problem into these three levels and use corresponding detailed annotations to facilitate preference learning.  The construction of the VistaDPO-7k dataset is also a significant contribution, as it provides a resource for fine-grained video-language preference learning that was previously unavailable.  Previous video DPO approaches (e.g. Hound-DPO) only considered coarse-grained alignment at the instance level. The incorporation of temporal and perceptive aspects, using manual annotations, represents a notable step forward. This combination of hierarchical optimization and a dedicated dataset represents a genuine advance.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper is multifaceted.

*   **Addressing a critical problem:** Video hallucination and misalignment are major roadblocks for the widespread adoption of LVMs.  VistaDPO directly tackles these issues.
*   **Performance improvements:** The experimental results show substantial performance gains on multiple benchmarks, indicating that VistaDPO is an effective solution.  The reported improvements over baselines, especially in the hallucination benchmarks, are compelling.
*   **Resource contribution:** The VistaDPO-7k dataset will likely be a valuable resource for the community, fostering further research in video-language alignment and DPO. It allows for more nuanced preference learning and evaluation.
*   **Potential for broader impact:** The hierarchical alignment approach could be generalized to other video-language tasks and even extended to other modalities beyond video.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly defined problem and solution:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and presents a well-defined and motivated solution.
*   **Rigorous methodology:** The construction of the VistaDPO-7k dataset is well-described, and the experimental setup is thorough.
*   **Strong empirical results:** The results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of VistaDPO on a variety of tasks and benchmarks.
*   **Publicly available resources:** The release of code and data increases the impact and reproducibility of the work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Annotation cost:** Manually annotating spatial-temporal groundings is expensive and time-consuming. This could limit the scalability of the approach to even larger datasets or different video domains.
*   **Limited Generalization analysis**:  While the performance on the benchmarks is impressive, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the model's ability to generalize to videos with significantly different characteristics than those found in the datasets used for training.
*   **Scope of Hierarchical decomposition:** While the 3 level hierarchy seems intuitively reasonable, a deeper exploration of alternative granularities, perhaps through ablation, could have made the contribution more robust.

**Potential Influence:**

VistaDPO has the potential to influence future research in several ways:

*   **Inspiring new alignment strategies:**  The hierarchical alignment approach could inspire researchers to explore other ways of decomposing the video-language alignment problem.
*   **Driving dataset development:**  The VistaDPO-7k dataset could serve as a benchmark for evaluating new alignment methods and inspire the creation of other fine-grained video-language datasets.
*   **Facilitating the development of more robust LVMs:**  By providing a more effective way to align video and language, VistaDPO could contribute to the development of LVMs that are less prone to hallucination and more aligned with human intuition.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **8**.  The paper presents a novel and effective approach to a critical problem in the field of LVMs.  The construction of a new dataset and the strong empirical results justify a high score. The limitations regarding annotation costs and potentially limited generalizability hold it back from being a truly exceptional contribution. The paper definitely makes a significant contribution to the field and provides a valuable resource for future research, warranting the assigned score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Low-hallucination Synthetic Captions for Large-Scale Vision-Language Model Pre-training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13123v1)
- **Authors**: Xinsong Zhang, Yarong Zeng, Xinting Huang, Hu Hu, Runquan Xie, Han Hu, Zhanhui Kang
- **Abstract**: In recent years, the field of vision-language model pre-training has experienced rapid advancements, driven primarily by the continuous enhancement of textual capabilities in large language models. However, existing training paradigms for multimodal large language models heavily rely on high-quality image-text pairs. As models and data scales grow exponentially, the availability of such meticulously curated data has become increasingly scarce and saturated, thereby severely limiting further advancements in this domain. This study investigates scalable caption generation techniques for vision-language model pre-training and demonstrates that large-scale low-hallucination synthetic captions can serve dual purposes: 1) acting as a viable alternative to real-world data for pre-training paradigms and 2) achieving superior performance enhancement when integrated into vision-language models through empirical validation. This paper presents three key contributions: 1) a novel pipeline for generating high-quality, low-hallucination, and knowledge-rich synthetic captions. Our continuous DPO methodology yields remarkable results in reducing hallucinations. Specifically, the non-hallucination caption rate on a held-out test set increases from 48.2% to 77.9% for a 7B-size model. 2) Comprehensive empirical validation reveals that our synthetic captions confer superior pre-training advantages over their counterparts. Across 35 vision language tasks, the model trained with our data achieves a significant performance gain of at least 6.2% compared to alt-text pairs and other previous work. Meanwhile, it also offers considerable support in the text-to-image domain. With our dataset, the FID score is reduced by 17.1 on a real-world validation benchmark and 13.3 on the MSCOCO validation benchmark. 3) We will release Hunyuan-Recap100M, a low-hallucination and knowledge-intensive synthetic caption dataset.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the critical data bottleneck in vision-language model (VLM) pre-training, specifically the scarcity of high-quality, aligned image-text pairs.  It proposes a novel pipeline for generating low-hallucination, knowledge-rich synthetic captions to overcome this limitation.  The approach uses Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to reduce hallucinations and supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to inject knowledge, leveraging large language models (LLMs) like GPT-4o and Qwen2-VL-7B.  The authors demonstrate empirically that VLMs pre-trained with their generated dataset, Hunyuan-Recap100M, achieve superior performance on various vision-language tasks compared to those trained on alt-text data or other synthetic captions.  They further validate the quality of their synthetic captions through both automated metrics and human evaluation. The dataset itself will be released for public use.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a well-engineered pipeline combining existing techniques (DPO, SFT) in a novel and effective manner for synthetic caption generation. The application of continuous DPO (CDPO), with iterative model refinement and data resampling, is a key contribution, addressing the scaling limitations of standard DPO.  The structured approach to knowledge injection, prioritizing entities/knowledge directly related to image content, adds to the novelty.  Compared to previous work, which often struggles with hallucination or lacks detailed descriptive content, this approach offers a tangible advancement.  However, the individual components (DPO, SFT) are not fundamentally new, the innovation lies in their orchestration and adaptation.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a very real and pressing problem in the VLM pre-training landscape. The ability to generate high-quality synthetic data at scale is crucial for continued progress.  If Hunyuan-Recap100M proves to be a widely adopted dataset, it could have a significant impact on VLM pre-training and downstream applications. The performance improvements reported across a range of tasks (especially those related to knowledge and hallucination) suggest a promising contribution.  The paper also provides a clear methodology, which should enable other researchers to build upon their approach. Releasing the dataset will facilitate further development in the vision-language community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Validation:** The paper provides extensive empirical evidence across numerous tasks (15 vision-language tasks, text-to-image evaluation, perceptual tests) to support their claims.
    *   **Clear Methodology:**  The pipeline is well-defined, detailing the specific models, prompts, and training procedures used.
    *   **Addresses a Key Problem:** The scarcity of high-quality data is a bottleneck, and the authors directly tackle this challenge.
    *   **Demonstrated Performance Gains:** Models pre-trained on their synthetic data outperform those trained on common alternatives.
    *   **Public Release of Dataset:** Will enable others to build on their work.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on GPT-4o:** The knowledge-enriching SFT stage depends on GPT-4o, which is a closed-source and computationally expensive resource. This raises concerns about reproducibility and accessibility.
    *   **Manual Review:** The need for manual review after the GPT-4o SFT stage indicates that the generated data is not perfect and requires human intervention. This limits the scalability of the method.
    *   **Hallucination Metric:** Although leveraging CIEM method, the use of GPT-4o to assess hallucination presents a black-box risk. It would be helpful to analyze *what types of* hallucinations are avoided and to use standard object detection methods to independently measure the existence of hallucinations.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the Hunyuan-Recap100M dataset proves its worth within the community, it could become a standard resource for VLM pre-training, stimulating further research on synthetic data generation and knowledge integration. The positive results in text-to-image generation also suggest that the dataset may be beneficial to cross-modal generative tasks.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

While the paper tackles a critical problem and demonstrates tangible improvements, the reliance on GPT-4o and manual review does impact scalability and reproducibility. The improvements are significant and the methodology is well articulated. Taking these factors into account, I assess that the contribution is noteworthy but not ground-breaking.

### LLMs Meet Finance: Fine-Tuning Foundation Models for the Open FinLLM Leaderboard
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13125v1)
- **Authors**: Varun Rao, Youran Sun, Mahendra Kumar, Tejas Mutneja, Agastya Mukherjee, Haizhao Yang
- **Abstract**: This paper investigates the application of large language models (LLMs) to financial tasks. We fine-tuned foundation models using the Open FinLLM Leaderboard as a benchmark. Building on Qwen2.5 and Deepseek-R1, we employed techniques including supervised fine-tuning (SFT), direct preference optimization (DPO), and reinforcement learning (RL) to enhance their financial capabilities. The fine-tuned models demonstrated substantial performance gains across a wide range of financial tasks. Moreover, we measured the data scaling law in the financial domain. Our work demonstrates the potential of large language models (LLMs) in financial applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LLMs Meet Finance: Fine-Tuning Foundation Models for the Open FinLLM Leaderboard" explores the application of large language models (LLMs) to financial tasks.  The authors fine-tune Qwen2.5 and Deepseek-R1 foundation models using the Open FinLLM Leaderboard as a benchmark. They employ techniques like supervised fine-tuning (SFT), direct preference optimization (DPO), and reinforcement learning (RL) to improve performance on a variety of financial tasks. The paper investigates data scaling laws in the financial domain and finds consistency with previous findings. The key contributions include fine-tuning LLMs for financial tasks, leveraging RL and chain-of-thought prompting, and measuring data scaling laws.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Application:** The paper directly addresses a relevant and growing area of research: applying LLMs to finance.  The use of the Open FinLLM Leaderboard provides a clear and standardized benchmark, which makes the results comparable and reproducible.
    *   **Comprehensive Methodology:** The combination of SFT, DPO, and RL techniques offers a well-rounded approach to fine-tuning, allowing the authors to address various challenges such as alignment, excessive length generation, and data scarcity.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results presented in the tables demonstrate the effectiveness of the fine-tuning methods and the consistency of data scaling laws in the financial domain. The comparisons with baseline models and different fine-tuning strategies add value.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology, experimental setup, and results. The figures and tables effectively illustrate the key findings.
    *   **Addressing Real-world Problems:** The paper acknowledges the challenges associated with applying LLMs in the financial sector, such as data availability and model alignment, and proposes practical solutions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper presents a thorough evaluation of existing techniques on financial tasks, the core methods (SFT, DPO, RL) are well-established. The novelty lies primarily in their application to the specific domain of finance using specific base models (Qwen2.5, Deepseek-R1). The improvements, while demonstrable, are incremental rather than breakthrough.
    *   **Limited Scope of RL Data Synthesis:** The data synthesis process for RL, while useful for tasks with limited training data, seems somewhat simplistic. A more sophisticated approach to generating realistic and diverse financial data could potentially lead to greater performance improvements.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** While the paper uses multiple models and tasks, there are limited detailed ablation studies beyond the SFT-DPO sequence to fully disentangle the effects of different techniques.  For example, a deeper dive into the effect of different RL reward functions, or a more detailed analysis of the chain-of-thought reasoning, would strengthen the analysis.
    *   **Model Size:** The paper focuses on relatively small 1.5B models. While this may be due to resource constraints, it limits the potential for achieving state-of-the-art performance. Experiments with larger models would be more convincing to show that these scaling laws can be replicated.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** While the consistency of data scaling laws across tasks is mentioned, there are only a limited number of specific financial tasks analysed in the work, leaving generalizability concerns for unseen task types.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper provides a valuable benchmark for applying LLMs to financial tasks, which can help guide future research and development in this area.
    *   The identification of data scaling laws in the financial domain is significant for understanding the relationship between data size and model performance, which can inform the development of more efficient and effective LLMs for financial applications.
    *   The practical insights gained from the fine-tuning experiments can be directly applied to improve the performance of LLMs in real-world financial applications.
    *   It has potential to further development in reinforcement learning and LLM approaches for financial tasks.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper represents a solid contribution to the field of applying LLMs to finance, but it falls short of being a groundbreaking work.  The application of well-established techniques to a specific domain, while useful, does not represent a high degree of novelty. The limitations of the data synthesis approach and the relatively small model size also detract from the overall impact. While the empirical validation is strong and the results are clearly presented, the lack of deeper ablation studies limits the understanding of the underlying mechanisms driving the performance improvements. Considering these factors, a score of 6 is appropriate.

- **Score**: 6/10

### Energy-Based Reward Models for Robust Language Model Alignment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13134v1)
- **Authors**: Anamika Lochab, Ruqi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Reward models (RMs) are essential for aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with human preferences. However, they often struggle with capturing complex human preferences and generalizing to unseen data. To address these challenges, we introduce Energy-Based Reward Model (EBRM), a lightweight post-hoc refinement framework that enhances RM robustness and generalization. EBRM models the reward distribution explicitly, capturing uncertainty in human preferences and mitigating the impact of noisy or misaligned annotations. It achieves this through conflict-aware data filtering, label-noise-aware contrastive training, and hybrid initialization. Notably, EBRM enhances RMs without retraining, making it computationally efficient and adaptable across different models and tasks. Empirical evaluations on RM benchmarks demonstrate significant improvements in both robustness and generalization, achieving up to a 5.97% improvement in safety-critical alignment tasks compared to standard RMs. Furthermore, reinforcement learning experiments confirm that our refined rewards enhance alignment quality, effectively delaying reward hacking. These results demonstrate our approach as a scalable and effective enhancement for existing RMs and alignment pipelines. The code is available at EBRM.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces Energy-Based Reward Models (EBRMs), a lightweight, post-hoc refinement framework designed to enhance the robustness and generalization capabilities of existing Reward Models (RMs) used in aligning Large Language Models (LLMs) with human preferences. EBRM models the reward distribution explicitly, capturing uncertainty and mitigating the impact of noisy or misaligned annotations through techniques like conflict-aware data filtering, label-noise-aware contrastive training, and hybrid initialization. Crucially, EBRM enhances RMs without requiring retraining, making it computationally efficient and adaptable across various models and tasks. The paper presents empirical results demonstrating significant improvements in robustness and generalization on RM benchmarks, and shows improved alignment quality via reinforcement learning experiments where refined rewards delay reward hacking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its application of energy-based models to the *post-hoc refinement* of reward models. While energy-based models are not new, and have even been explored in DPO settings, their use to *refine* existing RMs without retraining is relatively novel. The specific techniques for conflict-aware data filtering and label-noise-aware contrastive training contribute additional, albeit incremental, novelty. The hybrid initialization strategy also provides a practical contribution.

**Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to improve the reliability and robustness of LLM alignment, a critical area of research. The computational efficiency of EBRM, combined with its adaptability across different models and tasks, makes it a practical and scalable solution. The demonstrated improvements in safety-critical alignment and the delay in reward hacking are particularly significant. It is important to note, however, that the gains are *relative* and not absolute. The absolute numbers still imply that existing RMs, even enhanced with this, are still somewhat brittle and can be exploited. Further, the reliance on *proxy* rewards for training is a known limitation that is inherited by EBRM. The gains, while noticeable, are not revolutionary.

**Strengths:**

*   **Computational Efficiency:** EBRM's ability to enhance RMs without retraining is a major strength.
*   **Adaptability:** The framework is designed to be model-agnostic and task-agnostic.
*   **Improved Robustness and Generalization:** The empirical results demonstrate improvements in both areas.
*   **Demonstrated Delay in Reward Hacking:** The RL experiments suggest a more reliable reward signal.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Proxy Rewards:** The training process relies on the Base RM's output as a proxy for ground-truth rewards, inheriting any biases or limitations of the base model. While it does address this to some degree with noise addition, it does not solve the underlying problem.
*   **Incremental Gains:** While significant relative to the baseline, the absolute improvements are moderate, implying that further work is needed to achieve truly robust and reliable alignment. The degree to which the improvement on safety is significant might be debated.
*   **Limited Comparison:** The paper primarily compares against a limited set of baselines, particularly ensemble-based methods. A more extensive comparison with other state-of-the-art techniques would strengthen the evaluation.
*   **Noisy Annotations (in training):** While this paper attempts to "smooth" the labels, the *underlying annotations* could still be significantly biased and unrepresentative, which is not addressed.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Promoting the use of energy-based models for reward model refinement.
*   Providing a practical and scalable approach to improve LLM alignment.
*   Highlighting the importance of robustness and generalization in reward modeling.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

EBRM offers a novel and practical approach to improving reward models. The fact that it does *not* require retraining makes it a more sustainable solution for the community. The improvement in a safety scenario, while modest, is important and could prevent certain harms that RMs often miss. However, the reliance on "proxy" rewards during training, the "smoothing" approach instead of addressing the underlying noisy labels, and the limited absolute performance gains limits the scope of its influence. While the methodology is sound, the results suggest that EBRM alone is *not* a complete solution to robust reward modeling. The improvements over the baseline models is noteworthy and clearly stated in the findings.

### Syntactic and Semantic Control of Large Language Models via Sequential Monte Carlo
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13139v1)
- **Authors**: João Loula, Benjamin LeBrun, Li Du, Ben Lipkin, Clemente Pasti, Gabriel Grand, Tianyu Liu, Yahya Emara, Marjorie Freedman, Jason Eisner, Ryan Cotterel, Vikash Mansinghka, Alexander K. Lew, Tim Vieira, Timothy J. O'Donnell
- **Abstract**: A wide range of LM applications require generating text that conforms to syntactic or semantic constraints. Imposing such constraints can be naturally framed as probabilistic conditioning, but exact generation from the resulting distribution -- which can differ substantially from the LM's base distribution -- is generally intractable. In this work, we develop an architecture for controlled LM generation based on sequential Monte Carlo (SMC). Our SMC framework allows us to flexibly incorporate domain- and problem-specific constraints at inference time, and efficiently reallocate computational resources in light of new information during the course of generation. By comparing to a number of alternatives and ablations on four challenging domains -- Python code generation for data science, text-to-SQL, goal inference, and molecule synthesis -- we demonstrate that, with little overhead, our approach allows small open-source language models to outperform models over 8x larger, as well as closed-source, fine-tuned ones. In support of the probabilistic perspective, we show that these performance improvements are driven by better approximation to the posterior distribution. Our system builds on the framework of Lew et al. (2023) and integrates with its language model probabilistic programming language, giving users a simple, programmable way to apply SMC to a broad variety of controlled generation problems.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces an architecture based on Sequential Monte Carlo (SMC) for controlled text generation from Language Models (LMs). The framework aims to incorporate domain- and problem-specific constraints flexibly at inference time, reallocating computational resources dynamically based on new information. It contrasts with locally constrained decoding and sample reranking, arguing that SMC offers a better balance between exploiting immediate information and considering global constraints. The approach is evaluated on Python code generation, text-to-SQL, goal inference, and molecule synthesis, demonstrating that SMC can enable smaller LMs to outperform larger or fine-tuned models. The paper supports its claims with ablation studies and analyses of the KL divergence to show that performance improvements are driven by better approximations of the posterior distribution. The system integrates with a language model probabilistic programming framework, aiming for ease of use in various controlled generation problems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in the specific application of SMC to controlled text generation with a focus on programmable inference and integrating static/dynamic analyses for constraints. While SMC itself isn't new to language modeling (prior work exists on infilling and related tasks), the *specific* architectural specialization for code generation under syntactic/semantic constraints and the emphasis on programmability appear novel.  The integration of inexpensive/expensive potentials within the SMC framework is a relatively standard approach but is well-adapted here. The explicit linking of downstream performance to KL divergence approximation is also a valuable contribution.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem: effectively controlling the output of LMs, especially in safety-critical or domain-specific applications. The results demonstrating that smaller, open-source models can outperform larger ones are particularly important, as they suggest a path toward more accessible and controllable AI.  The claim about outperforming fine-tuned models, if robust, significantly enhances the claim of significance.  Furthermore, the provided benchmarks (Python code, SQL, etc.) address areas where there has been intensive research, thereby raising the bar. The modular approach and emphasis on programmability should make the proposed system readily adaptable to new applications.

**Strengths:**
*   The probabilistic framing is well-motivated and provides a principled way to approach the problem.
*   The SMC framework offers a flexible way to incorporate diverse constraints.
*   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, covering multiple domains and baselines.
*   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different algorithmic components.
*   The analysis of KL divergence helps to validate the probabilistic perspective.
*   The integration with a probabilistic programming language enhances the usability of the system.
*   The open source implementation greatly improves the reproducibility and extensibility of the results.

**Weaknesses:**
*   While the paper highlights the benefits of programmability, the actual implementation details are sparse. It does not detail the specifics of programmatically defining proposals and potential functions.
*   Some claims could be made more rigorous with additional analysis or experiments. For example, what are the limitations of SMC scaling? While the experiments are thorough, certain results are not compared statistically directly, which could enhance confidence.
*   The scalability of the approach to even *larger* language models or more complex constraints is not fully explored.
*   The computational cost analysis should be made more comprehensive across varying conditions to make general assertions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to controlled text generation by providing a principled framework and empirical evidence for its effectiveness. The core architectural approach isn't a radical departure, as it relies on existing techniques, but the *specific* application to code generation, the clear framing in terms of posterior approximation, and the empirical results on challenging benchmarks demonstrate real value and sets a high bar for comparison.  The claim about outperforming larger models adds substantial weight to the claim. The weaknesses are primarily related to limitations in detailed implementation information and some areas for additional empirical exploration. However, these do not significantly detract from the value of the work. It is an important step towards practical and controllable LMs.

### Sleep-time Compute: Beyond Inference Scaling at Test-time
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13171v1)
- **Authors**: Kevin Lin, Charlie Snell, Yu Wang, Charles Packer, Sarah Wooders, Ion Stoica, Joseph E. Gonzalez
- **Abstract**: Scaling test-time compute has emerged as a key ingredient for enabling large language models (LLMs) to solve difficult problems, but comes with high latency and inference cost. We introduce sleep-time compute, which allows models to "think" offline about contexts before queries are presented: by anticipating what queries users might ask and pre-computing useful quantities, we can significantly reduce the compute requirements at test-time. To demonstrate the efficacy of our method, we create modified versions of two reasoning tasks - Stateful GSM-Symbolic and Stateful AIME. We find that sleep-time compute can reduce the amount of test-time compute needed to achieve the same accuracy by ~ 5x on Stateful GSM-Symbolic and Stateful AIME and that by scaling sleep-time compute we can further increase accuracy by up to 13% on Stateful GSM-Symbolic and 18% on Stateful AIME. Furthermore, we introduce Multi-Query GSM-Symbolic, which extends GSM-Symbolic by including multiple related queries per context. By amortizing sleep-time compute across related queries about the same context using Multi-Query GSM-Symbolic, we can decrease the average cost per query by 2.5x. We then conduct additional analysis to understand when sleep-time compute is most effective, finding the predictability of the user query to be well correlated with the efficacy of sleep-time compute. Finally, we conduct a case-study of applying sleep-time compute to a realistic agentic SWE task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "sleep-time compute," a novel approach to improving the efficiency of Large Language Model (LLM) inference, particularly in stateful applications where context is persistent.  Instead of performing all inference at "test-time" (when a user makes a query), the paper proposes pre-computing useful quantities and inferences from the existing context while the model is idle ("sleep-time"). This reduces the amount of computation needed at test-time to answer user queries.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of this method by creating modified versions of existing mathematical reasoning tasks (GSM-Symbolic and AIME), introducing a multi-query extension, and by applying the technique to a realistic agentic software engineering task. The results show that sleep-time compute can significantly reduce test-time compute requirements, improve accuracy by scaling sleep-time computation, and decrease the average cost per query by amortizing sleep-time computation across multiple related queries. The paper also analyzes the conditions under which sleep-time compute is most effective, finding a correlation with query predictability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of "sleep-time compute" has a degree of novelty. While pre-computation and caching techniques are well-established in computer science, the specific application of pre-computing *reasoning and inference* using LLMs, leveraged on persistent context in stateful applications, is a valuable addition.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of sleep-time compute is significant. Reducing inference latency and cost is a crucial challenge for broader LLM adoption.  The paper targets a relevant problem and proposes a potentially viable solution.  The stateful context scenarios are realistic and represent common use cases for LLMs (e.g., document Q&A, coding assistants). Amortization across related queries is a key and valuable aspect.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined concept:** The paper clearly articulates the idea of sleep-time compute and provides a formal definition.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The experiments are well-designed and use appropriately modified benchmarks to demonstrate the benefits of sleep-time compute. The results are generally convincing, showing improvements in both accuracy and efficiency.
    *   **Realistic Case Study:** Applying the approach to SWE Features offers a more practical illustration of the technique's utility.
    *   **Analysis:**  The analysis of query predictability and its impact on the effectiveness of sleep-time compute is insightful.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Tasks:** The empirical evaluation focuses heavily on mathematical reasoning tasks. While useful for controlled experimentation, the applicability of sleep-time compute to other types of stateful LLM applications (e.g., dialogue systems, creative writing) requires further investigation. More task variety is necessary.
    *   **Synthetic Datasets:** Introducing Multi-Query GSM-Symbolic is helpful, but it's a synthetically generated dataset using LLMs. More naturally occurring multi-query datasets or user study data would strengthen the claims about amortization.
    *   **Heuristic for "Usefulness" of Inferences:**  The method for determining what inferences to pre-compute at sleep-time relies on prompting LLMs.  While this is a reasonable starting point, it might not be optimal. Better ways to ensure inferences that are truly useful at test-time may exist and need exploring. Relatedly, the prompts are quite simple, and more engineering effort could improve the results.
    *   **API/Implementation Details:** It would be helpful to provide concrete examples of what quantities are being pre-computed, in terms of the computational cost/size and what is being stored/indexed.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could stimulate further research into optimizing LLM inference by leveraging persistent contexts and pre-computation techniques. It provides a valuable framework and set of benchmarks for evaluating future approaches.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a promising approach to optimizing LLM inference in stateful applications. The novelty of the concept, supported by empirical validation, warrants recognition. However, the limited scope of the experimental evaluation and the reliance on LLMs for generating inferences at sleep-time prevent a higher score. The work provides a good starting point for future research, but needs to be expanded further in more areas and tasks.
Score: 6
The score reflects both the promise and limitations, and takes into consideration the need for further expansion of the approach beyond mathematical reasoning domains and the need to improve the methods for identifying beneficial inferences to be performed at sleep-time.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### It's All Connected: A Journey Through Test-Time Memorization, Attentional Bias, Retention, and Online Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13173v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Behrouz, Meisam Razaviyayn, Peilin Zhong, Vahab Mirrokni
- **Abstract**: Designing efficient and effective architectural backbones has been in the core of research efforts to enhance the capability of foundation models. Inspired by the human cognitive phenomenon of attentional bias-the natural tendency to prioritize certain events or stimuli-we reconceptualize neural architectures, including Transformers, Titans, and modern linear recurrent neural networks as associative memory modules that learn a mapping of keys and values using an internal objective, referred to as attentional bias. Surprisingly, we observed that most existing sequence models leverage either (1) dot-product similarity, or (2) L2 regression objectives as their attentional bias. Going beyond these objectives, we present a set of alternative attentional bias configurations along with their effective approximations to stabilize their training procedure. We then reinterpret forgetting mechanisms in modern deep learning architectures as a form of retention regularization, providing a novel set of forget gates for sequence models. Building upon these insights, we present Miras, a general framework to design deep learning architectures based on four choices of: (i) associative memory architecture, (ii) attentional bias objective, (iii) retention gate, and (iv) memory learning algorithm. We present three novel sequence models-Moneta, Yaad, and Memora-that go beyond the power of existing linear RNNs while maintaining a fast parallelizable training process. Our experiments show different design choices in Miras yield models with varying strengths. For example, certain instances of Miras achieve exceptional performance in special tasks such as language modeling, commonsense reasoning, and recall intensive tasks, even outperforming Transformers and other modern linear recurrent models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "It's All Connected: A Journey Through Test-Time Memorization, Attentional Bias, Retention, and Online Optimization" introduces MIRAS, a framework for designing sequence models. MIRAS is based on four key choices: memory architecture, attentional bias objective, retention gate, and memory learning algorithm. The paper argues that many existing sequence models can be understood as instances of associative memories with specific attentional biases (internal memory objectives).  It explores novel attentional bias configurations, retention mechanisms, and presents three new sequence model variants, MONETA, YAAD, and MEMORA. Experiments on language modeling, common-sense reasoning, and recall-intensive tasks demonstrate the potential of these new architectures, with some variants outperforming Transformers and other modern linear recurrent models. The paper also shows how existing RNNs can be framed as versions of this new framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the unifying MIRAS framework and the concept of attentional bias as an internal memory objective.  Reconceptualizing sequence models through this lens offers a fresh perspective and allows for systematic exploration of architectural choices. The specific novelties include the introduction of Lp norm attentional bias, Huber loss-based memory and Bregman Divergence-based retention gates. Also the three introduced models MONETA, YAAD, and MEMORA, provide new architectural options.
    *   However, a potential limitation is that while the framework is novel, the individual components (MLP layers, Norms, attention mechamisms etc) are not entirely new and some of these were seen before. The strength of the paper resides in its holistic understanding and providing a framework for them.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper depends on the long-term impact of the MIRAS framework. If it inspires further research and leads to the development of more efficient and effective sequence models, then it would be considered highly significant. The experimental results are promising, demonstrating the potential of the new architectures to outperform existing models in certain tasks. Furthermore, the study offers improved tools for memory managment.
    *   A potential weakness is that the experiments are not completely extensive, such as testing on a broad set of benchmarks and longer sequences. However, the chosen benchmarks can be considered representative.

*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly written. The theoretical background is explained in detail, and the experimental setup is well-described. The tables and figures are also helpful for understanding the results.

*   **Impact on the field:** The paper's impact on the field will depend on how widely the MIRAS framework is adopted and used by other researchers. If it leads to the development of new sequence models with improved performance, then it could have a significant impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-articulated framework for understanding and designing sequence models. The experimental results demonstrate the potential of the new architectures, and the theoretical background is sound.

However, as the introduced framework is novel there needs to be further analysis in the field to see its impact.

Therefore, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10
